Filter_2D

procedure Filter_2D
  (Source            : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination       : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Kernel            : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination_Depth : Filter_Depth := Same_Depth;
   Offset            : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 0.0;
   Border            : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Reflect_101)

Filter_2D replaces Destination with a linear correlation of Source against Kernel (native cv::filter2D). This is correlation, not mathematical convolution: the kernel is not mirrored around the anchor. For every channel,

Destination (x, y) = Offset + sum over kernel positions (i, j)
  of Kernel (i, j) * Source (x + i - Anchor.X, y + j - Anchor.Y)

where i is a kernel column, j a kernel row, and Anchor the kernel position aligned with the output pixel. This overload uses OpenCV's centered default anchor; there is no public (-1, -1) sentinel. Source must be a non-empty two-dimensional Mat of depth UInt8, UInt16, Int16, Float32, or Float64. Channel count is unrestricted; the same single-channel Kernel is applied independently to every Source channel. Kernel dimensions need not be odd, square, or normalized.

Destination receives Source's rows, columns, and channel count, with the requested depth, as a direct native output: storage that already has that size and type is reused (shallow copies and a Region's parent see the result); otherwise Destination is rebound to new storage. Direct in-place operation is supported when the requested destination depth matches Source; a depth-changing alias (Destination is Source or starts at its first element) is rejected. Source remains unchanged when Destination is distinct.

Parameters
Source

Image to filter.

Destination

Receives the filtered image.

Kernel

Correlation coefficients: a non-empty two-dimensional single-channel Float32 or Float64 Mat of any size. It must not share Destination's storage.

Destination_Depth

Output depth, Same_Depth by default; the supported source/destination combinations are those of Sobel and Laplacian (see Derivative_Depth).

Offset

Value added to each filtered value; must be finite.

Border

Extrapolation outside Source: Constant_Border, Replicate, Reflect, or Reflect_101 (default). Wrap is rejected.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D, or unsupported Source or Kernel, an unsupported depth combination, a nonfinite Offset, Wrap, a rejected alias, or a failure reported by OpenCV.

Filter_2D

procedure Filter_2D
  (Source            : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination       : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Kernel            : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Anchor            : OpenCV.Point;
   Destination_Depth : Filter_Depth := Same_Depth;
   Offset            : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 0.0;
   Border            : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Reflect_101)

This overload is the same Filter_2D correlation with an explicit Kernel Anchor. Anchor must lie inside Kernel: X and Y are nonnegative and strictly less than Kernel's columns and rows. There is no public mixed or centered (-1, -1) sentinel in this overload. Source, Kernel, depth, Offset, border, and direct native Destination rules match the centered overload. Direct in-place operation is supported when the requested destination depth matches Source.

Parameters
Source

Image to filter.

Destination

Receives the filtered image.

Kernel

Non-empty two-dimensional single-channel Float32 or Float64 correlation coefficients; must not share Destination's storage.

Anchor

Kernel position aligned with each output pixel: column X and row Y within Kernel.

Destination_Depth

Output depth, Same_Depth by default; see Derivative_Depth for the supported combinations.

Offset

Value added to each filtered value; must be finite.

Border

Extrapolation outside Source: Constant_Border, Replicate, Reflect, or Reflect_101 (default). Wrap is rejected.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an Anchor outside Kernel, any input rejected by the centered overload, or a failure reported by OpenCV.

Filter_Depth

subtype Filter_Depth is Derivative_Depth;

Destination depth of Filter_2D and Sep_Filter_2D, with the literals and supported Source combinations of Derivative_Depth.

Find_Ballard_Template_Matches

function Find_Ballard_Template_Matches
  (Template       : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Scene          : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Vote_Threshold : Hough_Vote_Threshold := 20;
   Canny_Low      : Positive := 50;
   Canny_High     : Positive := 100) return Ballard_Template_Match_Array

Finds translated copies of Template in Scene with OpenCV's generalized Hough Ballard detector, one match per accepted accumulator peak. Translation-only native Ballard on nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1 images: fixed levels=360, dp=1, minDist=1, scale=1, angle=0. Rotation and scale search is Find_Guil_Template_Matches.

Inputs are unchanged; Regions are isolated logical images. Both inputs are copied into private packed snapshots before preprocessing, so parent pixels never take part and results use Region-local coordinates. Native preprocessing is Canny (3x3 aperture, L1 magnitude) plus Float32 Sobel derivatives, with no silent threshold scaling. Template edges form an orientation-indexed R-table relative to the Template center (Columns / 2, Rows / 2); a Template without edges yields an empty result. Centers are Scene-local pixels. Votes must strictly exceed Vote_Threshold, and a peak must also satisfy the native local-maximum conditions; several matches may be returned.

Invalid inputs or unsafe native arithmetic raise OpenCV_Error. The arithmetic restrictions are derived from the native code, not arbitrary caps; for example a Scene of more than 268435454 pixels (16384 x 16384) is rejected because its possible peak count would overflow native output conversion. Synchronous and costly for large edge sets: many Template edges in one orientation bin multiply the work at each Scene edge, and private images, derivatives, a Scene-sized histogram and result buffers are allocated.

Parameters
Template

Shape to find: a nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1 grayscale image whose Canny edges build the R-table.

Scene

Image to search: a nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1 grayscale image. Result centers are in its pixel coordinates.

Vote_Threshold

Accumulator votes that a match must strictly exceed; default 20.

Canny_Low

Lower Canny threshold, used as given; it must be less than Canny_High. Default 50.

Canny_High

Upper Canny threshold, used as given; default 100.

Return Value

Fresh Ada array of detections. Nonempty bounds start at zero; empty is 1 .. 0.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D or non-UInt8 C1 Template or Scene, Canny_Low >= Canny_High, unsafe native arithmetic, an unrepresentable decoded vote, or an OpenCV failure.

Find_Contours

function Find_Contours
  (Source        : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Retrieval     : Contour_Retrieval_Mode := External_Only;
   Approximation : Contour_Approximation_Mode := Simple;
   Offset        : OpenCV.Point := (X => 0, Y => 0)) return Contour_Set

Extracts the contours of a binary image with cv::findContours. Zero Source pixels are background and nonzero pixels are foreground. Contour points lie on foreground boundary pixels (a filled rectangle yields its corner pixels under Simple), shifted by Offset. Contours and hierarchy are copied into the returned Ada-owned Contour_Set and the native result is released. Indices are zero-based. An all-zero Source returns an empty set.

Parameters
Source

Binary image: a nonempty two-dimensional UInt8 C1 Mat. It remains unchanged.

Retrieval

Which contours are returned and how their hierarchy entries are linked; defaults to External_Only.

Approximation

How the boundary points of each contour are stored; defaults to Simple.

Offset

Added to every returned point, for example to express contours of a Region in its parent's coordinates. Hierarchy links are unaffected. Defaults to (0, 0).

Return Value

A fresh Ada-owned Contour_Set, independent of Source and of native storage.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised when Source is empty, not two-dimensional, not UInt8 or not single-channel, when a native contour index or count is invalid or exceeds the C ABI range, and when OpenCV fails.

Find_Guil_Template_Matches

function Find_Guil_Template_Matches
  (Template   : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Scene      : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Parameters : Guil_Search_Parameters :=
     (Canny_Low          => 50,
      Canny_High         => 100,
      Minimum_Angle      => 0.0,
      Maximum_Angle      => 360.0,
      Angle_Step         => 5.0,
      Minimum_Scale      => 0.5,
      Maximum_Scale      => 2.0,
      Scale_Step         => 0.05,
      Angle_Threshold    => 100,
      Scale_Threshold    => 100,
      Position_Threshold => 20)) return Guil_Template_Match_Array

Finds rotated and scaled copies of Template in Scene with OpenCV's generalized Hough Guil detector. Each match reports a Scene-local center, a native positive scale, an angle in degrees and three native vote counts; Find_Ballard_Template_Matches is the translation-only alternative.

Native Guil on nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1 images. Inputs are unchanged. Regions are isolated logical images: both inputs are copied into private packed snapshots, so parent pixels never take part. Template center is (Columns / 2, Rows / 2). Centers are Scene-local pixels. Angle_Degrees is degrees, never radians, and is not normalized by this binding. Scale is the native positive estimate. Votes are decoded from Int32, never through Float32. A hypothesis is kept only when each vote count meets or exceeds its threshold. Fixed levels=360, xi=90 degrees, angle epsilon=1 degree, dp=1, minimum distance=1 (no native peak-grid filtering), and 1000 features per orientation bin; none of these is a public option.

Parameters must satisfy 0 <= Minimum_Angle < Maximum_Angle <= 360, 0 < Angle_Step < 360, 0 < Minimum_Scale < Maximum_Scale, and Scale_Step > 0. All floats must be finite, and 0 < Canny_Low < Canny_High. Invalid inputs or unsafe native arithmetic raise OpenCV_Error: searches whose native contour area, histogram index, per-cell vote or output-copy width cannot be represented are rejected. In particular the angle-bin count times the scale-bin count times the Scene area must not exceed 134217727 (INT_MAX / 16), so the default 72 x 30 bins admit Scenes of at most 62137 pixels; narrower windows admit larger Scenes. Feature construction is quadratic in edge count and repeats across accepted angle/scale hypotheses, so a dense high-resolution edge image is costly.

Parameters
Template

Shape to find: a nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1 grayscale image whose Canny edges build the features.

Scene

Image to search: a nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1 grayscale image. Result centers are in its pixel coordinates.

Parameters

Canny thresholds, angle and scale windows and vote thresholds; see Guil_Search_Parameters for meanings and defaults.

Return Value

Fresh Ada array of detections. Nonempty bounds start at zero; empty is 1 .. 0.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D or non-UInt8 C1 Template or Scene, a nonfinite or out-of-range parameter, Canny_Low >= Canny_High, unsafe native arithmetic, an invalid native result, or an OpenCV failure.

Find_Hough_Circle_Centers

function Find_Hough_Circle_Centers
  (Source                  : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Accumulator_Scale       : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Minimum_Center_Distance : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Canny_Threshold         : Hough_Circle_Threshold;
   Accumulator_Threshold   : Hough_Circle_Threshold;
   Minimum_Radius          : OpenCV.Size_Coordinate := 0)
   return Hough_Circle_Center_Array

Find_Hough_Circle_Centers runs the classic gradient detector with the same Source and parameter contract as Find_Hough_Circles, but skips radius estimation and reports only candidate centers. Minimum_Radius still limits how close to each edge pixel center votes are cast, and OpenCV's automatic maximum (the larger Source dimension) still bounds the voting walk. It uses OpenCV's private centers-only mode, never exposed, and discards OpenCV's placeholder radius. Because no radius is estimated, the radius-estimation limits of Find_Hough_Circles (the 46340 maximum radius and a nonempty radius histogram) do not apply; the accumulator and fixed-point limits do.

Parameters
Source

Grayscale image to analyse: nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1.

Accumulator_Scale

Inverse accumulator resolution (OpenCV dp), finite and at least 1.0.

Minimum_Center_Distance

Minimum distance between detected centers (OpenCV minDist); positive and finite.

Canny_Threshold

Upper threshold of the internal Canny edge detector; the lower one is half of it, but at least one.

Accumulator_Threshold

Accumulator vote threshold for circle centers.

Minimum_Radius

Smallest radius, in pixels, at which center votes are cast from each edge pixel; default 0.

Return Value

Fresh Ada array of centers in Source pixel coordinates, in unspecified order. Nonempty results start at zero; when no center is found the result is the null range 1 .. 0.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D or non-UInt8 C1 Source, an invalid Accumulator_Scale or Minimum_Center_Distance, native accumulator arithmetic that cannot be represented, or an OpenCV failure.

Find_Hough_Circles

function Find_Hough_Circles
  (Source                  : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Accumulator_Scale       : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Minimum_Center_Distance : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Canny_Threshold         : Hough_Circle_Threshold;
   Accumulator_Threshold   : Hough_Circle_Threshold;
   Minimum_Radius          : OpenCV.Size_Coordinate := 0)
   return Hough_Circle_Array

Find_Hough_Circles runs OpenCV's classic gradient Hough circle detector (HOUGH_GRADIENT) on a nonempty two-dimensional UInt8 C1 grayscale Source; it computes its own Sobel/Canny edges, so blurring the input first usually improves results. Accumulator_Scale is the inverse accumulator resolution and must be finite and at least 1.0 (OpenCV would silently clamp smaller values; this API rejects them). Minimum_Center_Distance must be positive and finite. Canny_Threshold is the upper Canny threshold (the lower one is half of it, but at least one). Accumulator_Threshold is the center-vote threshold; OpenCV also applies it to radius support when estimating radii.

This overload searches radii from Minimum_Radius up to an automatic maximum chosen by OpenCV (the larger Source dimension). Radius estimation squares that maximum in native int, so a Source with more than 46340 rows or columns is rejected. Also rejected: a radius range that yields no radius-histogram bin at this Accumulator_Scale (for example Minimum_Radius not below the larger Source dimension), an accumulator whose padded size exceeds native int indexing, and (larger Source dimension + Minimum_Radius + 2) * 1024 beyond native int range (the fixed-point voting walk).

Parameters
Source

Grayscale image to analyse: nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1.

Accumulator_Scale

Inverse accumulator resolution (OpenCV dp), finite and at least 1.0; the value 1.0 keeps Source resolution.

Minimum_Center_Distance

Minimum distance between detected centers (OpenCV minDist); positive and finite.

Canny_Threshold

Upper threshold of the internal Canny edge detector; the lower one is half of it, but at least one.

Accumulator_Threshold

Accumulator vote threshold for circle centers, also applied to radius support.

Minimum_Radius

Smallest searched radius in pixels; default 0. With the automatic maximum it must leave a nonempty radius range.

Return Value

Fresh Ada array of circles in unspecified order. Nonempty results start at zero; when no circle is found the result is the null range 1 .. 0.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D or non-UInt8 C1 Source, an invalid Accumulator_Scale or Minimum_Center_Distance, an empty radius range, native arithmetic that cannot be represented, or an OpenCV failure.

Find_Hough_Circles

function Find_Hough_Circles
  (Source                  : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Accumulator_Scale       : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Minimum_Center_Distance : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Canny_Threshold         : Hough_Circle_Threshold;
   Accumulator_Threshold   : Hough_Circle_Threshold;
   Minimum_Radius          : OpenCV.Size_Coordinate := 0;
   Maximum_Radius          : OpenCV.Size_Coordinate)
   return Hough_Circle_Array

This overload of Find_Hough_Circles has the same HOUGH_GRADIENT detector, Source, threshold and result contract as the automatic-maximum overload, but searches the explicit radius interval Minimum_Radius .. Maximum_Radius. Maximum_Radius must exceed Minimum_Radius; it is never silently widened (OpenCV would rewrite an invalid maximum to Minimum_Radius + 2). Maximum_Radius must be at most 46340, because radius estimation squares it in native int, and the interval must yield at least one radius-histogram bin at this Accumulator_Scale. The accumulator and fixed-point limits of the other overload also apply.

Parameters
Source

Grayscale image to analyse: nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1.

Accumulator_Scale

Inverse accumulator resolution (OpenCV dp), finite and at least 1.0.

Minimum_Center_Distance

Minimum distance between detected centers (OpenCV minDist); positive and finite.

Canny_Threshold

Upper threshold of the internal Canny edge detector; the lower one is half of it, but at least one.

Accumulator_Threshold

Accumulator vote threshold for circle centers, also applied to radius support.

Minimum_Radius

Smallest searched radius in pixels; default 0.

Maximum_Radius

Largest searched radius in pixels; greater than Minimum_Radius and at most 46340.

Return Value

Fresh Ada array of circles in unspecified order. Nonempty results start at zero; when no circle is found the result is the null range 1 .. 0.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D or non-UInt8 C1 Source, an invalid Accumulator_Scale or Minimum_Center_Distance, Maximum_Radius <= Minimum_Radius, an empty radius range, native arithmetic that cannot be represented, or an OpenCV failure.

Find_Hough_Circles_With_Votes

function Find_Hough_Circles_With_Votes
  (Source                  : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Accumulator_Scale       : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Minimum_Center_Distance : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Canny_Threshold         : Hough_Circle_Threshold;
   Accumulator_Threshold   : Hough_Circle_Threshold;
   Minimum_Radius          : OpenCV.Size_Coordinate := 0)
   return Hough_Circle_With_Votes_Array

Find_Hough_Circles_With_Votes mirror the two Find_Hough_Circles overloads (automatic and explicit maximum radius) and add the support count of each estimated radius. They always estimate radii and never use the centers-only mode, whose native fourth component is an accumulator index rather than a vote count. This overload has the contract of the automatic-maximum Find_Hough_Circles: radii from Minimum_Radius up to the larger Source dimension, which must be at most 46340, with the same rejections. Votes come from OpenCV's binary32 Vec4f output: integer counts that may already have been rounded when above 2**24.

Parameters
Source

Grayscale image to analyse: nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1.

Accumulator_Scale

Inverse accumulator resolution (OpenCV dp), finite and at least 1.0.

Minimum_Center_Distance

Minimum distance between detected centers (OpenCV minDist); positive and finite.

Canny_Threshold

Upper threshold of the internal Canny edge detector; the lower one is half of it, but at least one.

Accumulator_Threshold

Accumulator vote threshold for circle centers, also applied to radius support.

Minimum_Radius

Smallest searched radius in pixels; default 0. With the automatic maximum it must leave a nonempty radius range.

Return Value

Fresh Ada array of circles with votes, in unspecified order. Nonempty results start at zero; when no circle is found the result is the null range 1 .. 0.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D or non-UInt8 C1 Source, an invalid Accumulator_Scale or Minimum_Center_Distance, an empty radius range, native arithmetic that cannot be represented, or an OpenCV failure.

Find_Hough_Circles_With_Votes

function Find_Hough_Circles_With_Votes
  (Source                  : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Accumulator_Scale       : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Minimum_Center_Distance : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Canny_Threshold         : Hough_Circle_Threshold;
   Accumulator_Threshold   : Hough_Circle_Threshold;
   Minimum_Radius          : OpenCV.Size_Coordinate := 0;
   Maximum_Radius          : OpenCV.Size_Coordinate)
   return Hough_Circle_With_Votes_Array

This overload of Find_Hough_Circles_With_Votes mirrors the explicit-interval Find_Hough_Circles: radii from Minimum_Radius to Maximum_Radius, which must exceed Minimum_Radius (never silently widened) and be at most 46340, with the same rejections. Like the other overload it always estimates radii and reports, for each circle, the support count of the chosen radius from OpenCV's binary32 Vec4f output (counts above 2**24 may already have been rounded).

Parameters
Source

Grayscale image to analyse: nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1.

Accumulator_Scale

Inverse accumulator resolution (OpenCV dp), finite and at least 1.0.

Minimum_Center_Distance

Minimum distance between detected centers (OpenCV minDist); positive and finite.

Canny_Threshold

Upper threshold of the internal Canny edge detector; the lower one is half of it, but at least one.

Accumulator_Threshold

Accumulator vote threshold for circle centers, also applied to radius support.

Minimum_Radius

Smallest searched radius in pixels; default 0.

Maximum_Radius

Largest searched radius in pixels; greater than Minimum_Radius and at most 46340.

Return Value

Fresh Ada array of circles with votes, in unspecified order. Nonempty results start at zero; when no circle is found the result is the null range 1 .. 0.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D or non-UInt8 C1 Source, an invalid Accumulator_Scale or Minimum_Center_Distance, Maximum_Radius <= Minimum_Radius, an empty radius range, native arithmetic that cannot be represented, or an OpenCV failure.

Find_Hough_Line_Segments

function Find_Hough_Line_Segments
  (Source                   : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Distance_Resolution      : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Angle_Resolution_Radians : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Vote_Threshold           : Hough_Vote_Threshold;
   Minimum_Line_Length      : OpenCV.Size_Coordinate := 0;
   Maximum_Line_Gap         : OpenCV.Size_Coordinate := 0)
   return Hough_Line_Segment_Array

Find_Hough_Line_Segments runs the probabilistic Hough transform on the same binary Source contract as Find_Hough_Lines (zero is background, every nonzero pixel a candidate), without angle bounds. Minimum_Line_Length and Maximum_Line_Gap are integer pixel counts, because OpenCV rounds them to integers. A segment is kept when its X or Y extent reaches Minimum_Line_Length; up to Maximum_Line_Gap missing pixels may be bridged along a line. The native walk uses a fixed internal random seed, but endpoints may still differ by a pixel or two across OpenCV builds, so compare them with a small tolerance.

Requests whose native int accumulator or fixed-point walk arithmetic would overflow are rejected: the Source limits of Find_Hough_Lines, a resolution whose accumulator sizing exceeds native int range, a Distance_Resolution too coarse for the accumulator, and any Source with more than 32765 rows or columns (OpenCV 4.1 and 4.10 walk segments in 16.16 fixed point; the limit applies on every version).

Parameters
Source

Binary image to analyse: nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1, with every nonzero pixel a candidate.

Distance_Resolution

Rho bin width in pixels; positive and finite.

Angle_Resolution_Radians

Angle bin width in radians; positive and finite.

Vote_Threshold

Accumulator vote threshold for the candidate lines of the probabilistic walk.

Minimum_Line_Length

Shortest kept segment, as its X or Y extent in pixels; default 0.

Maximum_Line_Gap

Largest bridged run of missing pixels along a line; default 0.

Return Value

Fresh Ada array of segments in unspecified order, each with unspecified endpoint order. Nonempty results start at zero; when no segment is found the result is the null range 1 .. 0.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D or non-UInt8 C1 Source, a nonpositive or nonfinite resolution, native accumulator or walk arithmetic that cannot be represented, or an OpenCV failure.

Find_Hough_Lines

function Find_Hough_Lines
  (Source                   : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Distance_Resolution      : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Angle_Resolution_Radians : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Vote_Threshold           : Hough_Vote_Threshold;
   Minimum_Angle_Radians    : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 0.0;
   Maximum_Angle_Radians    : OpenCV.Float64_Value := Ada.Numerics.Pi)
   return Hough_Line_Array

Find_Hough_Lines runs the classical standard Hough transform on a nonempty two-dimensional UInt8 C1 binary Source: zero pixels are background and every nonzero pixel (not only 255) is a candidate. Distance_Resolution (pixels) and Angle_Resolution_Radians must be positive and finite. The angle bounds must be finite with 0 <= Minimum_Angle_Radians < Maximum_Angle_Radians <= Pi. OpenCV keeps accumulator bins that are strict local maxima with more than Vote_Threshold votes. Each result is an accumulator bin, so Rho and Angle_Radians are quantized by the requested resolutions; expect agreement with exact geometry within about one bin. OpenCV 5.0 reconstructs Rho with integer arithmetic where OpenCV 4.x uses a half-bin offset, so Rho can differ by up to half a distance bin between generations. Multiscale srn/stn, OpenCV 5 edge-value weighting, and accumulator votes are not exposed; Find_Hough_Lines_With_Votes reports votes.

OpenCV narrows the resolutions and angle bounds to binary32 and sizes its accumulator with signed int arithmetic. Requests that would overflow it are rejected: a Source whose pixel count or 2 * (Rows + Columns) + 1 exceeds native int range, a resolution whose bin counts or accumulator size exceed native int range, a Distance_Resolution too coarse for the accumulator's vote columns, or a nonzero-pixel count times angle-bin count beyond native int range (the IPP line estimate, checked on every build). There is no arbitrary resolution floor.

Parameters
Source

Binary image to analyse: nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1, with every nonzero pixel a candidate.

Distance_Resolution

Rho bin width in pixels; positive and finite.

Angle_Resolution_Radians

Angle bin width in radians; positive and finite.

Vote_Threshold

Accumulator votes that a reported line must strictly exceed.

Minimum_Angle_Radians

Lowest searched angle, in radians; finite, at least 0.0 and below Maximum_Angle_Radians. Default 0.0.

Maximum_Angle_Radians

Highest searched angle, in radians; finite and at most Pi. Default Pi.

Return Value

Fresh Ada array of lines in unspecified order. Nonempty results start at zero; when no line is found the result is the null range 1 .. 0.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D or non-UInt8 C1 Source, a nonpositive or nonfinite resolution, invalid angle bounds, native accumulator arithmetic that cannot be represented, or an OpenCV failure.

Find_Hough_Lines_From_Points

function Find_Hough_Lines_From_Points
  (Points                   : Hough_Point_Array;
   Maximum_Lines            : Positive;
   Vote_Threshold           : Hough_Vote_Threshold;
   Minimum_Rho              : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Maximum_Rho              : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Distance_Resolution      : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Minimum_Angle_Radians    : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 0.0;
   Maximum_Angle_Radians    : OpenCV.Float64_Value := Ada.Numerics.Pi;
   Angle_Resolution_Radians : OpenCV.Float64_Value := Hough_Degree)
   return Hough_Line_With_Votes_Array

Find_Hough_Lines_From_Points runs OpenCV's HoughLinesPointSet on an explicit set of points (any bounds; may be empty; every coordinate finite). Rho bins start at Minimum_Rho with width Distance_Resolution; Minimum_Rho < Maximum_Rho is required, and every point must vote inside the rho range for every searched angle, otherwise the request is rejected (this makes OpenCV 4.1, which does not bounds-check votes, behave safely). Since abs (Rho) <= sqrt (X**2 + Y**2), a range reaching a little beyond plus and minus the largest point distance from the origin always works. Angle bounds satisfy 0 <= Minimum_Angle_Radians < Maximum_Angle_Radians <= Pi, and each resolution must yield at least one bin: both (Maximum_Rho - Minimum_Rho + 1) / Distance_Resolution and the angle span divided by Angle_Resolution_Radians must be at least 1. At most Maximum_Lines results are returned; an empty point set returns the empty result without calling OpenCV. Votes come from OpenCV's Vec3d output, so they are exact integer counts. Rho bounds must lie within binary32 range, and requests whose native bin counts, accumulator indexing or binary32 vote arithmetic cannot be represented are rejected.

Parameters
Points

Points that vote, as binary32 coordinates; any bounds, possibly empty, every coordinate finite.

Maximum_Lines

Upper bound on the number of returned lines, a cap rather than a requested count.

Vote_Threshold

Accumulator vote threshold; OpenCV keeps lines whose votes exceed it.

Minimum_Rho

Start of the first rho bin and lower rho bound, in point coordinate units; finite and below Maximum_Rho.

Maximum_Rho

Upper rho bound, in point coordinate units; finite.

Distance_Resolution

Rho bin width; positive and finite.

Minimum_Angle_Radians

Lowest searched angle, in radians; finite, at least 0.0 and below Maximum_Angle_Radians. Default 0.0.

Maximum_Angle_Radians

Highest searched angle, in radians; finite and at most Pi. Default Pi.

Angle_Resolution_Radians

Angle bin width in radians; positive and finite. Default Hough_Degree.

Return Value

Fresh Ada array of lines with votes, in unspecified order. Nonempty results start at zero; when no line is found the result is the null range 1 .. 0.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for a nonfinite point coordinate, invalid or nonfinite rho or angle bounds, a resolution that is not positive and finite or yields no bin, a point voting outside the rho range, native arithmetic that cannot be represented, or an OpenCV failure.

Find_Hough_Lines_With_Votes

function Find_Hough_Lines_With_Votes
  (Source                   : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Distance_Resolution      : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Angle_Resolution_Radians : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Vote_Threshold           : Hough_Vote_Threshold;
   Minimum_Angle_Radians    : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 0.0;
   Maximum_Angle_Radians    : OpenCV.Float64_Value := Ada.Numerics.Pi)
   return Hough_Line_With_Votes_Array

Find_Hough_Lines_With_Votes has exactly the contract of Find_Hough_Lines and adds each line's accumulator votes. OpenCV's optional IPP standard-Hough path is available only to the vote-free output, so on IPP-enabled builds the two operations need not return identical lines or ordering. Votes come from OpenCV's binary32 Vec3f output: integer counts, normally above Vote_Threshold, which may already have been rounded when above 2**24.

Parameters
Source

Binary image to analyse: nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1, with every nonzero pixel a candidate.

Distance_Resolution

Rho bin width in pixels; positive and finite.

Angle_Resolution_Radians

Angle bin width in radians; positive and finite.

Vote_Threshold

Accumulator votes that a reported line must strictly exceed.

Minimum_Angle_Radians

Lowest searched angle, in radians; finite, at least 0.0 and below Maximum_Angle_Radians. Default 0.0.

Maximum_Angle_Radians

Highest searched angle, in radians; finite and at most Pi. Default Pi.

Return Value

Fresh Ada array of lines with votes, in unspecified order. Nonempty results start at zero; when no line is found the result is the null range 1 .. 0.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for the same conditions as Find_Hough_Lines: an invalid Source, resolution or angle bound, native accumulator arithmetic that cannot be represented, or an OpenCV failure.

Fixed_Remap_Maps

type Fixed_Remap_Maps is private;

Native fixed-point absolute remap maps, created by Convert_Remap_To_Fixed for repeated use with the fixed-map Remap. Coordinates are Int16 C2; Coefficients are UInt16 C1 for interpolation or empty for nearest-only maps. The invariant-bearing pair is private, so neither part can be mutated independently. A default-initialized value holds no maps (Is_Empty) and is rejected by Remap and by the conversions back to Float32.

Float_Remap_Maps

type Float_Remap_Maps is record
   Map_X : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Map_Y : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
end record;

A pair of separate absolute Float32 C1 remap maps, as accepted by the separate-map Remap and returned by Separate_Remap_Map and Convert_Remap_To_Separate_Float (both components then fresh owning storage of identical geometry).

Record fields
Map_X

Source column (X) coordinate for each Destination pixel.

Map_Y

Source row (Y) coordinate for each Destination pixel.

Flood_Fill

procedure Flood_Fill
  (Image            : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Seed             : OpenCV.Point;
   New_Value        : OpenCV.Scalar;
   Result           : out Flood_Fill_Result;
   Lower_Difference : OpenCV.Scalar := (others => 0.0);
   Upper_Difference : OpenCV.Scalar := (others => 0.0);
   Connectivity     : Pixel_Connectivity := Four_Connected;
   Range_Mode       : Flood_Fill_Range_Mode := Floating_Range)

Fills the connected component containing Seed with New_Value in place. Image must be a nonempty two-dimensional UInt8, Int32, or Float32 Mat with one or three channels; Seed (X is the column, Y the row) is inside it. A neighbour joins the component when every channel lies within reference - Lower_Difference .. reference + Upper_Difference, where the reference follows Range_Mode. The components used by Image's channels must be finite, and the differences nonnegative; unused components are ignored. A Region is filled as its own logical image and mutates its parent's storage; shallow aliases observe the mutation. Validation failures leave Image unchanged.

Int32 requires each channel's stored maximum minus minimum <= INT_MAX to prevent native signed subtraction overflow (including mask-only). Active Int32 New_Value lies in Int32_Value'First .. Int32_Value'Last; differences lie in 0 .. Int32_Value'Last. Native rounding of New_Value and flooring of differences preserve fractional Scalar semantics. Float32 Images need the components used within the finite Float32 range; UInt8 components must not exceed INT_MAX in magnitude. Native segment coordinates limit Image to 65535 rows and 65535 columns, and its pixel count and row-offset arithmetic must fit a signed int.

Parameters
Image

Image filled in place.

Seed

Starting pixel, inside Image.

New_Value

Replacement value in channel order; components beyond Image's channel count are ignored.

Result

Receives the filled pixel count and bounds; assigned only on success.

Lower_Difference

Nonnegative per-channel amount a neighbour may lie below the reference; defaults to zero.

Upper_Difference

Nonnegative per-channel amount a neighbour may lie above the reference; defaults to zero.

Connectivity

Neighbourhood used for growth; defaults to Four_Connected.

Range_Mode

Choice of reference value; defaults to Floating_Range.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D or unsupported Image, a Seed outside Image, a nonfinite or out-of-range component in use, a negative difference, an Int32 span or native size limit, or an OpenCV failure.

Flood_Fill_Mask_Value

subtype Flood_Fill_Mask_Value is OpenCV.UInt8_Value range 1 .. 255;

Nonzero byte that Flood_Fill_With_Mask writes into its Mask for every filled pixel; a fill enters only zero mask pixels.

Flood_Fill_Range_Mode

type Flood_Fill_Range_Mode is (Floating_Range, Fixed_Range);

Reference value that a flood-fill candidate pixel is compared with. Floating_Range compares each candidate with its already-filled neighbour; Fixed_Range compares every candidate with the seed pixel.

Enumeration Literal
Floating_Range

Compare with the adjacent pixel already in the component, so the fill can follow a gradual ramp.

Fixed_Range

Compare with the seed pixel, so every filled pixel lies within the differences of the seed value.

Flood_Fill_Result

type Flood_Fill_Result is record
   Pixel_Count : Natural;
   Bounds      : OpenCV.Rect;
end record;

Outcome of a successful flood fill, also reported in mask-only mode. Pixel_Count is the number of filled pixels. Bounds is the smallest rectangle containing them, in the coordinates of the supplied Image.

Record fields
Pixel_Count

Number of pixels in the filled component.

Bounds

Smallest rectangle containing the filled pixels, in the supplied Image's coordinates (a Region's own coordinates).

Flood_Fill_With_Mask

procedure Flood_Fill_With_Mask
  (Image            : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Mask             : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Seed             : OpenCV.Point;
   New_Value        : OpenCV.Scalar;
   Result           : out Flood_Fill_Result;
   Lower_Difference : OpenCV.Scalar := (others => 0.0);
   Upper_Difference : OpenCV.Scalar := (others => 0.0);
   Connectivity     : Pixel_Connectivity := Four_Connected;
   Range_Mode       : Flood_Fill_Range_Mode := Floating_Range;
   Mask_Fill_Value  : Flood_Fill_Mask_Value := 1;
   Mask_Only        : Boolean := False)

As Flood_Fill, additionally constrained and recorded by Mask. Mask is a UInt8 C1 Mat of (Image.Rows + 2) x (Image.Columns + 2) that must not share storage with Image: Image (X, Y) corresponds to Mask (X + 1, Y + 1). Filling never crosses a nonzero mask pixel. Every filled pixel is set to Mask_Fill_Value in Mask, and OpenCV sets Mask's one-pixel outer border to 1. When Mask_Only is True, Image is left unchanged and New_Value is ignored (not even validated); Mask and Result are still updated. Mask must be nonempty and is modified in place.

Image, Seed, difference, connectivity, Region, Int32 and native-limit rules are those of Flood_Fill; the Int32 span rule also applies in mask-only mode. Validation failures leave Image and Mask unchanged, including the mask border.

Parameters
Image

Image filled in place; unchanged when Mask_Only is True.

Mask

UInt8 C1 guide and record of the fill, two rows and two columns larger than Image, updated in place.

Seed

Starting pixel, inside Image.

New_Value

Replacement value in channel order; ignored when Mask_Only is True.

Result

Receives the filled pixel count and bounds; assigned only on success.

Lower_Difference

Nonnegative per-channel amount a neighbour may lie below the reference; defaults to zero.

Upper_Difference

Nonnegative per-channel amount a neighbour may lie above the reference; defaults to zero.

Connectivity

Neighbourhood used for growth; defaults to Four_Connected.

Range_Mode

Choice of reference value; defaults to Floating_Range.

Mask_Fill_Value

Byte written into Mask for each filled pixel; defaults to 1.

Mask_Only

When True, only Mask and Result are updated; defaults to False.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for any Flood_Fill rejection, an empty or non-UInt8-C1 Mask, a Mask not two rows and columns larger than Image, Mask storage shared with Image, or an OpenCV failure.

Font_Scale_For_Height

function Font_Scale_For_Height
  (Pixel_Height : Positive;
   Font         : Text_Font := Hershey_Simplex;
   Thickness    : Drawing_Thickness := 1) return OpenCV.Float64_Value

Returns the font scale at which Font, drawn with Thickness, has the requested pixel height, as computed by cv::getFontScaleFromHeight. Reject heights where 2 * Pixel_Height <= Thickness + 1 (the OpenCV 4 scale would be nonpositive), regardless of the linked OpenCV version; the comparison uses widened arithmetic. A nonfinite or nonpositive native scale, or one outside the Draw_Text preflight bounds for a single character, is also rejected. Text measured at the returned scale can differ from Pixel_Height by a few pixels because of native rounding and generation-specific font metrics.

Parameters
Pixel_Height

Desired text height in pixels, comparable with the Size.Height reported by Measure_Text.

Font

Font selector; defaults to Hershey_Simplex.

Thickness

Intended glyph stroke thickness; defaults to 1.

Return Value

Positive finite scale, usable as Font_Scale.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised when 2 * Pixel_Height <= Thickness + 1, when the native scale is not finite and positive or fails the preflight, and when OpenCV fails.

Gabor_Kernel_Depth

Gaussian_Blur

procedure Gaussian_Blur
  (Source      : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Kernel_Size : OpenCV.Size;
   Sigma       : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Border      : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Reflect_101)

Gaussian_Blur convolves Source with an isotropic Gaussian kernel (native cv::GaussianBlur): the same Sigma applies along X and Y. Source must be a non-empty two-dimensional Mat with depth UInt8, UInt16, Int16, Float32, or Float64. Destination receives Source's rows, columns, depth, and channel count as a direct native output: storage that already has that size and type is reused (shallow copies and a Region's parent see the result); otherwise Destination is rebound to new storage.

Parameters
Source

Image to smooth.

Destination

Receives the smoothed image.

Kernel_Size

Kernel width and height in pixels; both positive and odd.

Sigma

Gaussian standard deviation in pixels, used for both axes; positive and finite.

Border

Extrapolation outside Source: Constant_Border, Replicate, Reflect, or Reflect_101 (default). Wrap is rejected.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D, or unsupported-depth Source, a zero or even kernel dimension, a nonpositive or nonfinite Sigma, Wrap, or a failure reported by OpenCV.

Gaussian_Kernel_Depth

type Gaussian_Kernel_Depth is (Float32_Kernel, Float64_Kernel);

Float64_Kernel

Number of coefficients of a Get_Gaussian_Kernel vector. The generator requires an odd value; with automatic sigma, 1 yields the single coefficient 1.0.

Get_Derivative_Kernels

function Get_Derivative_Kernels
  (X_Order       : Derivative_Order;
   Y_Order       : Derivative_Order;
   Kernel_Size   : Sobel_Kernel_Size := Kernel_3;
   Normalization : Derivative_Kernel_Normalization := Unnormalized;
   Depth         : Derivative_Kernel_Depth := Float32_Kernel)
   return Derivative_Kernels

Get_Derivative_Kernels generates the separable 1-D Sobel-family coefficient vectors used by spatial derivatives (native cv::getDerivKernels); it does not filter an image itself. Kernel_X is the horizontal/X-direction vector and Kernel_Y is the vertical/Y-direction vector. Both are independently owned fresh single-channel column vectors (N x 1) of matching Float32 or Float64 depth. Coefficient order is preserved; the generator does not flip coefficients. The pair may be passed directly to Sep_Filter_2D:

Kernels :=
  Get_Derivative_Kernels
    (X_Order     => 1,
     Y_Order     => 0,
     Kernel_Size => Kernel_3);
Sep_Filter_2D
  (Source,
   Destination,
   Kernel_X          => Kernels.Kernel_X,
   Kernel_Y          => Kernels.Kernel_Y,
   Destination_Depth => Float32_Depth);

That request yields Kernel_X [-1, 0, 1] and Kernel_Y [1, 2, 1]. Kernel_3, Kernel_5, and Kernel_7 use that kernel size in both directions. Kernel_1 uses OpenCV's special derivative behavior: a nonzero-order direction has effective length 3, while a zero-order direction remains a 1-tap identity, so Kernel_X and Kernel_Y lengths may differ. There is no public FILTER_SCHARR or -1 kernel-size sentinel; use Get_Scharr_Kernels for Scharr coefficients.

Parameters
X_Order

Derivative order along X; strictly less than the effective Kernel_X length.

Y_Order

Derivative order along Y; strictly less than the effective Kernel_Y length. X_Order and Y_Order cannot both be zero.

Kernel_Size

Sobel aperture selecting the vector lengths; Kernel_3 by default.

Normalization

Coefficient scaling; Normalized changes scaling, not derivative order. Unnormalized by default.

Depth

Depth of both vectors; Float32_Kernel by default.

Return Value

Fresh Kernel_X/Kernel_Y coefficient pair.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised when both orders are zero, an order is not less than its effective length, or OpenCV fails.

Get_Gabor_Kernel

function Get_Gabor_Kernel
  (Kernel_Size          : OpenCV.Size;
   Sigma                : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Orientation_Radians  : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Wavelength           : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Aspect_Ratio         : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Phase_Offset_Radians : OpenCV.Float64_Value :=
     OpenCV.Float64_Value (Ada.Numerics.Pi / 2.0);
   Depth                : Gabor_Kernel_Depth := Float64_Kernel)
   return OpenCV.Core.Mat

Generates a Gabor filter kernel (native cv::getGaborKernel) for use with Filter_2D: a fresh owning C1 Mat of the requested Depth with exactly Kernel_Size.Height rows and Kernel_Size.Width columns. For integer offsets x, y from the center, theta = Orientation_Radians, lambda = Wavelength, gamma = Aspect_Ratio and psi = Phase_Offset_Radians, each coefficient is

xr = x * cos (theta) + y * sin (theta)
yr = -x * sin (theta) + y * cos (theta)
v  = exp (-(xr**2 + (gamma * yr)**2) / (2 * Sigma**2)) *
       cos (2 * pi * xr / lambda + psi)

Native automatic sizing is deliberately not exposed. Coefficients are not normalized, and the binding adds no reversal or transposition: native coordinates (x, y) are stored at row Height / 2 - y and column Width / 2 - x, OpenCV's 180-degree coordinate reversal, which also affects the apparent phase. Extreme finite parameters retain native IEEE behavior and may produce nonfinite coefficients through derived expressions; Float32 narrowing can lose precision.

Parameters
Kernel_Size

Kernel width and height in pixels; both positive and odd. There is no automatic-size sentinel.

Sigma

Standard deviation of the Gaussian envelope along xr, in pixels; positive and finite.

Orientation_Radians

Angle theta of the rotated xr axis, along which the sinusoid varies; finite radians, not normalized.

Wavelength

Wavelength lambda of the sinusoidal factor, in pixels; positive and finite.

Aspect_Ratio

Spatial aspect ratio gamma: the envelope's standard deviation along yr is Sigma / Aspect_Ratio; positive and finite.

Phase_Offset_Radians

Phase offset psi of the sinusoid; finite radians, not normalized. Defaults to pi / 2.

Depth

Coefficient depth; Float64_Kernel by default.

Return Value

Fresh Height x Width coefficient Mat.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for a zero or even dimension, a nonpositive or nonfinite Sigma, Wavelength or Aspect_Ratio, a nonfinite angle, an allocation size limit, or a native failure.

Get_Gaussian_Kernel

function Get_Gaussian_Kernel
  (Kernel_Size : Gaussian_Kernel_Size;
   Sigma       : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Depth       : Gaussian_Kernel_Depth := Float64_Kernel)
   return OpenCV.Core.Mat

This overload is the same Get_Gaussian_Kernel generator with an explicit Sigma: it returns a fresh owning N x 1 single-channel vector of symmetric Gaussian coefficients of the requested Depth, in OpenCV's order. Zero and negative values are rejected rather than switching to automatic sigma. Used as both Sep_Filter_2D kernels, it gives the Gaussian_Blur result for the same size and Sigma, up to rounding.

Parameters
Kernel_Size

Number of coefficients N; must be odd.

Sigma

Gaussian standard deviation in coefficient (pixel) units; must be finite and strictly greater than zero.

Depth

Coefficient depth; Float64_Kernel by default.

Return Value

Fresh N x 1 coefficient vector.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an even Kernel_Size, a zero, negative or nonfinite Sigma, or a failure reported by OpenCV.

Get_Gaussian_Kernel

function Get_Gaussian_Kernel
  (Kernel_Size : Gaussian_Kernel_Size;
   Depth       : Gaussian_Kernel_Depth := Float64_Kernel)
   return OpenCV.Core.Mat

Get_Gaussian_Kernel returns an N x 1 single-channel Gaussian coefficient vector of the requested Depth (native cv::getGaussianKernel), where N is Kernel_Size, as fresh owning storage. This overload asks OpenCV to derive sigma from Kernel_Size; there is no public zero or negative sigma sentinel. Coefficients preserve OpenCV's values and ordering: they are symmetric and sum to 1 within rounding, and Kernel_Size 1 yields exactly 1.0. The vector may be passed directly to Sep_Filter_2D as Kernel_X and Kernel_Y.

Parameters
Kernel_Size

Number of coefficients N; must be odd.

Depth

Coefficient depth; Float64_Kernel by default.

Return Value

Fresh N x 1 coefficient vector.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an even Kernel_Size or a failure reported by OpenCV.

Get_Histogram_Bin_Boundaries

Get_Histogram_Dimension

function Get_Histogram_Dimension
  (Value : Histogram; Index : Positive) return Histogram_Dimension

Index is 1 .. Histogram_Dimension_Count; others raise OpenCV_Error. The returned dimension does not include Source_Position. For a nonuniform axis this is the envelope: Bin_Count is the number of intervals, Lower_Bound is the first stored edge, and Upper_Bound is the last. It is not a claim that the intervals are equal.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Get_Scharr_Kernels

function Get_Scharr_Kernels
  (Axis          : Derivative_Axis;
   Normalization : Derivative_Kernel_Normalization := Unnormalized;
   Depth         : Derivative_Kernel_Depth := Float32_Kernel)
   return Derivative_Kernels

Get_Scharr_Kernels generates the separable 1-D Scharr first-derivative coefficient pair for Axis. It does not filter an image itself. Both returned vectors are independently owned fresh 3 x 1 single-channel Mats of matching Float32 or Float64 depth. Coefficient order is preserved and may be passed directly to Sep_Filter_2D. X_Axis maps to X_Order 1 / Y_Order 0, giving Kernel_X [-1, 0, 1] and the smoothing vector Kernel_Y [3, 10, 3]; Y_Axis maps to X_Order 0 / Y_Order 1 and swaps the roles. Normalized changes smoothing-vector scaling (to [3, 10, 3] / 32), not the derivative vector or order. The native Scharr kernel-size sentinel remains private.

Parameters
Axis

Direction of the first derivative.

Normalization

Smoothing-vector scaling; Unnormalized by default.

Depth

Depth of both vectors; Float32_Kernel by default.

Return Value

Fresh Kernel_X/Kernel_Y coefficient pair, each of length 3.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for a failure reported by OpenCV.

Get_Structuring_Element

function Get_Structuring_Element
  (Kernel_Size : OpenCV.Size; Shape : Morphology_Shape := Rectangle)
   return OpenCV.Core.Mat

Fresh UInt8 C1 mask with native 0/1 values and exact positive size, for use as the Kernel of Erode, Dilate and Apply_Morphology. Values are 1 for included and 0 for excluded pixels, never 255. Even and non-square dimensions are supported. Size (1, 1) is always Rectangle. Only Cross geometry depends on Anchor; Mat retains no anchor metadata. Pass the intended anchor again when applying a custom Cross kernel. The centered overload uses (Width / 2, Height / 2). Ellipse height is limited to 92681; native allocation limits also apply.

Parameters
Kernel_Size

Mask width (columns) and height (rows); both must be positive.

Shape

Generated shape; defaults to Rectangle.

Return Value

Fresh owning UInt8 C1 mask of exactly Kernel_Size.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised when a dimension is zero, when the Ellipse or allocation limits are exceeded, or when OpenCV fails.

Get_Structuring_Element

function Get_Structuring_Element
  (Kernel_Size : OpenCV.Size;
   Shape       : Morphology_Shape;
   Anchor      : OpenCV.Point) return OpenCV.Core.Mat

As the centered overload, but builds the mask around an explicit Anchor, which must lie inside the Kernel_Size box; no negative center sentinel exists. Rectangle and Ellipse masks ignore Anchor; a Cross includes the row and column through Anchor. The returned Mat does not record Anchor, so pass the same Anchor to the morphology operation.

Parameters
Kernel_Size

Mask width (columns) and height (rows); both must be positive.

Shape

Generated shape; no default in this overload.

Anchor

Kernel position, inside the mask, through which a Cross passes.

Return Value

Fresh owning UInt8 C1 mask of exactly Kernel_Size.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised when a dimension is zero, when Anchor lies outside the mask, when the Ellipse or allocation limits are exceeded, or when OpenCV fails.

GrabCut_Label

type GrabCut_Label is
  (Definite_Background,
   Definite_Foreground,
   Probable_Background,
   Probable_Foreground);

Per-pixel GrabCut label, stored in the state mask with OpenCV's numeric encoding (see GrabCut_Label_Value). Segmentation never changes a definite label and moves probable pixels only between the two probable labels.

Enumeration Literal
Definite_Background

Value 0: fixed background.

Definite_Foreground

Value 1: fixed foreground.

Probable_Background

Value 2: likely background, re-estimated by segmentation.

Probable_Foreground

Value 3: likely foreground, re-estimated by segmentation.

GrabCut_Minimum_Training_Pixels

GrabCut_Minimum_Training_Pixels : constant := 5;

Minimum background and foreground pixel counts required to initialize GrabCut portably: OpenCV 4.1 clusters each training set into five Gaussian-mixture components with k-means, which needs five samples. OpenCV 4.10 and 5.0 would reduce the component count instead. Restore_GrabCut_State does not apply this minimum.

GrabCut_Model_Column_Count

GrabCut_Model_Column_Count : constant Positive := 65;

Columns of each Float64 C1 1 x 65 native GrabCut mixture model: five component weights (columns 0 .. 4), 15 means (5 .. 19), and five 3 x 3 covariance matrices (20 .. 64). The layout is specific to OpenCV GrabCut and the same in OpenCV 4.1, 4.10 and 5.0.

GrabCut_State

type GrabCut_State is limited private;

GrabCut segmentation state: a private label mask plus hidden background and foreground colour models. The type is limited, so a state cannot be shallow-copied. A default-initialized state is uninitialized and is rejected by every operation except Is_Initialized. Each state exclusively owns its Mats, which are released by normal Mat finalization; two states never share mutable storage.

Guil_Search_Parameters

type Guil_Search_Parameters is record
   Canny_Low          : Positive := 50;
   Canny_High         : Positive := 100;
   Minimum_Angle      : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 0.0;
   Maximum_Angle      : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 360.0;
   Angle_Step         : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 5.0;
   Minimum_Scale      : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 0.5;
   Maximum_Scale      : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 2.0;
   Scale_Step         : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 0.05;
   Angle_Threshold    : Positive := 100;
   Scale_Threshold    : Positive := 100;
   Position_Threshold : Positive := 20;
end record;

Canny thresholds, angle and scale search windows, and vote thresholds of Find_Guil_Template_Matches. The defaults (Canny 50/100, angles 0 .. 360 by 5 degrees, scales 0.5 .. 2.0 by 0.05, vote thresholds 100/100/20) are convenient starting values, not measured recommendations; a narrow window is much faster and less ambiguous. All floats must be finite. Native histogram bin counts are ceil ((Maximum - Minimum) * (1 / Step)), the reciprocal product rather than direct division, and must fit native int arithmetic.

Record fields
Canny_Low

Lower Canny threshold, used as given; 0 < Canny_Low < Canny_High.

Canny_High

Upper Canny threshold, used as given.

Minimum_Angle

Lowest searched rotation in degrees; 0.0 <= Minimum_Angle < Maximum_Angle.

Maximum_Angle

Highest searched rotation in degrees; at most 360.0 degrees.

Angle_Step

Angle histogram step in degrees; greater than 0.0 and less than 360.0.

Minimum_Scale

Lowest searched scale factor; 0.0 < Minimum_Scale < Maximum_Scale.

Maximum_Scale

Highest searched scale factor.

Scale_Step

Scale histogram step; must be positive.

Angle_Threshold

Angle votes that a hypothesis must meet or exceed.

Scale_Threshold

Scale votes that a hypothesis must meet or exceed.

Position_Threshold

Position votes that a hypothesis must meet or exceed.

Guil_Template_Match

type Guil_Template_Match is record
   Center         : OpenCV.Float32_Point;
   Scale          : OpenCV.Float32_Value;
   Angle_Degrees  : OpenCV.Float32_Value;
   Position_Votes : Natural;
   Scale_Votes    : Natural;
   Angle_Votes    : Natural;
end record;

One Find_Guil_Template_Matches detection: the Scene position, scale and rotation of a Template instance, with the native support of each.

Record fields
Center

Scene-local position, in pixels, of the integer Template center (Template.Columns / 2, Template.Rows / 2); finite and nonnegative.

Scale

Native positive scale estimate of the instance relative to Template (1.0 is unscaled); finite and not renormalized.

Angle_Degrees

Native rotation estimate in degrees, never radians; finite and not normalized by this binding.

Position_Votes

Native Int32 position votes, decoded exactly; at least the Position_Threshold used.

Scale_Votes

Native Int32 scale votes, decoded exactly; at least the Scale_Threshold used.

Angle_Votes

Native Int32 angle votes, decoded exactly; at least the Angle_Threshold used.

Guil_Template_Match_Array

  array (Natural range <>) of Guil_Template_Match;

Result of Find_Guil_Template_Matches. Nonempty results are zero-based; an empty result has range 1 .. 0. Element order is not a ranking.

Harris_Corner_Response

function Harris_Corner_Response
  (Source     : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Block_Size : Corner_Block_Size := 3;
   Aperture   : Corner_Aperture := Aperture_3;
   K          : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 0.04;
   Border     : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Reflect_101)
   return OpenCV.Core.Mat

Harris corner response map (native cv::cornerHarris), computed from the Block_Size covariance of Sobel derivatives with Harris free parameter K. Source requirements, snapshot and Region isolation, border support and native limits are those of Corner_Minimum_Eigenvalue.

Parameters
Source

Nonempty 2-D UInt8 or Float32 C1 image to analyse.

Block_Size

Covariance neighborhood side length; at least 2. Default 3.

Aperture

Sobel derivative aperture; default Aperture_3.

K

Harris detector free parameter; must be finite. Default 0.04.

Border

Constant_Border, Replicate, Reflect or Reflect_101 (the default); Wrap is rejected.

Return Value
