Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Unsupported Source type or layout, Block_Size 1, nonfinite K, Wrap border, native size limits, or an OpenCV failure.

Histogram

type Histogram is private;

A dense histogram of Float32 bin counts together with the source, channel, and bin-boundary metadata it was calculated with. A Histogram is immutable: copies share no mutable state and no operation exposes its storage. A default-initialized Histogram is empty (zero dimensions, Uniform_Binning) and is rejected by Compare_Histograms, Back_Project, and Accumulate_Histogram.

Histogram_Bin_Boundary_Array

type Histogram_Bin_Boundary_Array is
  array (Positive range <>) of OpenCV.Float32_Value;

Explicit nonuniform bin edges. Iteration order is the edge sequence; the array's index bounds are irrelevant. N edges define N - 1 bins [Edges (I), Edges (I + 1)). At least two edges are required.

Histogram_Bin_Count

subtype Histogram_Bin_Count is Positive range 1 .. 2_147_483_647;

Number of bins on one histogram axis. In addition, the product of the bin counts of all axes of one histogram must not exceed 2_147_483_647; a larger product raises OpenCV_Error when the histogram is calculated.

Histogram_Binning

function Histogram_Binning
  (Value : Histogram) return Histogram_Binning_Mode

Returns the binning mode shared by every axis of Value. A default-initialized Histogram reports Uniform_Binning.

Parameters
Value

Histogram to inspect.

Return Value

Uniform_Binning or Nonuniform_Binning.

Histogram_Binning_Mode

type Histogram_Binning_Mode is (Uniform_Binning, Nonuniform_Binning);

Every axis of one Histogram uses one binning mode. Uniform axes divide [Lower_Bound, Upper_Bound) into equal bins. Nonuniform axes use the stored explicit edges.

Enumeration Literal
Uniform_Binning

Each axis divides its range into Bin_Count equal bins; produced by Calculate_Histogram and reported by a default-initialized Histogram.

Nonuniform_Binning

Each axis uses its stored explicit edge sequence; produced by Calculate_Nonuniform_Histogram.

Histogram_Comparison_Method

type Histogram_Comparison_Method is
  (Correlation,
   Chi_Square,
   Intersection,
   Hellinger_Distance,
   Alternative_Chi_Square,
   Kullback_Leibler_Divergence);

OpenCV comparison metrics. None is a normalized similarity score. In the formulas, Left and Right are corresponding bin values and each sum runs over all bins; zero bins follow native OpenCV handling.

Enumeration Literal
Correlation

Correlation coefficient of the bin values: larger is more similar and identical histograms give 1.

Chi_Square

sum ((Left - Right) ** 2 / Left): smaller is closer and identical gives 0; asymmetric (Left is the denominator).

Intersection

sum (min (Left, Right)), the sum of bin minima: larger is more similar and the scale depends on the histogram totals (identical histograms give their total count).

Hellinger_Distance

OpenCV HISTCMP_BHATTACHARYYA, which computes the Hellinger distance; smaller is closer and identical gives 0.

Alternative_Chi_Square

Symmetric chi-square 2 * sum ((Left - Right) ** 2 / (Left + Right)); smaller is closer and identical gives 0.

Kullback_Leibler_Divergence

sum (Left * log (Left / Right)): smaller is closer and identical gives 0; asymmetric.

Histogram_Dimension

type Histogram_Dimension is record
   Channel     : Natural;
   Bin_Count   : Histogram_Bin_Count;
   Lower_Bound : OpenCV.Float32_Value;
   Upper_Bound : OpenCV.Float32_Value;
end record;

One uniform histogram axis of a single source. Channel is zero-based relative to that Source Mat. Bin_Count equal-width bins cover the half-open range [Lower_Bound, Upper_Bound); samples below Lower_Bound or at or above Upper_Bound are not counted. Both bounds must be finite with Lower_Bound < Upper_Bound. Internally this is source position 0.

Record fields
Channel

Zero-based channel of Source sampled on this axis; it must be below Source.Channels. Several axes may select one channel.

Bin_Count

Number of equal-width bins on this axis.

Lower_Bound

Finite inclusive lower edge of the first bin.

Upper_Bound

Finite exclusive upper edge of the last bin, greater than Lower_Bound.

Histogram_Dimension_Array

type Histogram_Dimension_Array is
  array (Positive range <>) of Histogram_Dimension;

Dimension order is iteration order; any index lower bound is accepted.

Histogram_Dimension_Count

function Histogram_Dimension_Count (Value : Histogram) return Natural

Returns the number of axes of Value: 1 .. Maximum_Histogram_Dimensions for a calculated histogram and 0 for a default-initialized one.

Parameters
Value

Histogram to inspect.

Return Value

The stored dimension count.

Histogram_Nonuniform_Dimension

type Histogram_Nonuniform_Dimension is private;

One nonuniform axis. The descriptor owns a copy of Boundaries, so the caller's array may be modified or go out of scope afterwards. Edges must be finite and strictly increasing. They are not required to lie inside the numeric range of a particular source depth. Descriptors are built, and their edges validated, by Nonuniform_Histogram_Dimension and Nonuniform_Histogram_Source_Dimension.

Histogram_Nonuniform_Dimension_Array

type Histogram_Nonuniform_Dimension_Array is
  array (Positive range <>) of Histogram_Nonuniform_Dimension;

Dimension order is iteration order; any index lower bound is accepted. Every axis of one histogram uses this nonuniform mode. Mixed uniform and nonuniform axes are not supported.

Histogram_Source_Dimension

type Histogram_Source_Dimension is record
   Source_Position : Natural;
   Channel         : Natural;
   Bin_Count       : Histogram_Bin_Count;
   Lower_Bound     : OpenCV.Float32_Value;
   Upper_Bound     : OpenCV.Float32_Value;
end record;

One uniform axis of a multi-source histogram. Source_Position is the zero-based position in Mat_Array iteration order, independent of the array's lower bound: position 0 is Sources'First. Channel is zero-based within that selected source. Native concatenated channel numbers are not part of this type.

Record fields
Source_Position

Zero-based position of the sampled source in Sources iteration order; it must be below Sources'Length.

Channel

Zero-based channel within the selected source; it must be below that source's channel count.

Bin_Count

Number of equal-width bins on this axis.

Lower_Bound

Finite inclusive lower edge of the first bin.

Upper_Bound

Finite exclusive upper edge of the last bin, greater than Lower_Bound.

Histogram_Source_Dimension_Array

type Histogram_Source_Dimension_Array is
  array (Positive range <>) of Histogram_Source_Dimension;

Dimension order is iteration order; any index lower bound is accepted.

Histogram_Values

function Histogram_Values (Value : Histogram) return OpenCV.Core.Mat

Returns an independent deep copy of the Float32 C1 bin counts. A one-dimensional histogram is a Bin_Count x 1 Mat, a two-dimensional one is Bin_Count (1) x Bin_Count (2), and higher dimensionality is an N-dimensional Mat with one extent per dimension, in dimension order. The metadata, not the Mat shape, defines the logical dimensionality. The one-dimensional Bin_Count x 1 shape is the same on every supported OpenCV release.

Parameters
Value

Histogram whose bin counts are copied.

Return Value

Fresh owning Float32 C1 Mat of bin counts; changing it never affects Value.

Hit_Or_Miss

procedure Hit_Or_Miss
  (Source      : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Kernel      : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Iterations  : Morphology_Iterations := 1;
   Border      : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Constant_Border)

Native Hit-or-Miss returns a UInt8 C1 0/255 match mask of Source size. Source must be nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1 with exactly 0/255 pixels. Kernel must be nonempty 2-D Int8 C1: +1 requires foreground, -1 background, and 0 is don't-care. All-zero kernels deliberately reject native copy behavior. Hit-only and miss-only kernels are supported. Violations raise OpenCV_Error. No conversion/thresholding is performed. Center is (Kernel.Columns / 2, Kernel.Rows / 2), even for even sizes. Iterations erodes each hit/complemented-miss leg N times, then ANDs once; it does not repeat the complete transform. Supported borders are Constant_Border, Replicate, Reflect and Reflect_101, not Wrap. Both legs use the same native default morphology border policy, not separately complemented external values: with Constant_Border, outside pixels satisfy both +1 and -1 entries, so matches touching the edge can appear that Replicate would not produce. There is no Border_Value parameter.

Destination is a direct native output with Source's rows and columns and type UInt8 C1: OpenCV writes into its storage when it already has that size and type, so shallow copies and a Region's parent see the result, and otherwise rebinds it to new storage. Invalid public input is rejected before native execution. Regions are isolated logical images. Direct in-place operation, including a Region, is supported; Kernel/Destination storage overlap rejects. Distinct Source remains unchanged. Only the logical view of a Kernel Region is inspected. The morphology signed-int limits apply; the expanded-rectangle limit concerns only kernels that are entirely +1 or entirely -1.

Parameters
Source

Binary image of exactly 0 (background) and 255 (foreground) pixels.

Destination

Receives the 0/255 match mask.

Kernel

Pattern of +1 (foreground), -1 (background) and 0 (don't-care) entries, with at least one nonzero entry.

Iterations

Erosions applied to each leg before the single AND; defaults to 1.

Border

Extrapolation of pixels outside Source; defaults to Constant_Border.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised when Source is not a nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1 image of 0/255 values, when Kernel is not a nonempty 2-D Int8 C1 pattern of -1/0/+1 with a nonzero entry, for Wrap, Kernel/Destination overlap or a native arithmetic limit, or when OpenCV fails.

Hit_Or_Miss

procedure Hit_Or_Miss
  (Source      : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Kernel      : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Anchor      : OpenCV.Point;
   Iterations  : Morphology_Iterations := 1;
   Border      : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Constant_Border)

As the centered Hit_Or_Miss, but the pattern is anchored at Anchor, where (0, 0) is the top-left kernel element. Explicit Anchor is inside Kernel: no negative center sentinel. Source and Kernel requirements, Iterations, borders, Destination, in-place, Region and limit behavior are those of the centered overload.

Parameters
Source

Binary image of exactly 0 (background) and 255 (foreground) pixels.

Destination

Receives the 0/255 match mask (direct native output with Source's rows and columns).

Kernel

Pattern of +1 (foreground), -1 (background) and 0 (don't-care) entries, with at least one nonzero entry.

Anchor

Kernel position aligned with each tested pixel; it must lie inside Kernel.

Iterations

Erosions applied to each leg before the single AND; defaults to 1.

Border

Extrapolation of pixels outside Source; defaults to Constant_Border.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised when Source is not a nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1 image of 0/255 values, when Kernel is not a nonempty 2-D Int8 C1 pattern of -1/0/+1 with a nonzero entry, when Anchor lies outside Kernel, for Wrap, Kernel/Destination overlap or a native arithmetic limit, or when OpenCV fails.

Hough_Circle

type Hough_Circle is record
   Center : OpenCV.Float32_Point;
   Radius : OpenCV.Float32_Value;
end record;

A gradient-Hough circle. Center and Radius are native binary32 pixel values quantized by the accumulator scale: centers come from accumulator cells and radii from histogram estimates, so compare them with a tolerance of a pixel or two rather than exactly.

Record fields
Center

Circle center in Source pixel coordinates.

Radius

Estimated circle radius in pixels.

Hough_Circle_Array

type Hough_Circle_Array is array (Natural range <>) of Hough_Circle;

Result of Find_Hough_Circles. Nonempty results are zero-based; an empty result has range 1 .. 0.

Hough_Circle_Center_Array

type Hough_Circle_Center_Array is
  array (Natural range <>) of OpenCV.Float32_Point;

Centers found by center-only gradient-Hough detection (Find_Hough_Circle_Centers), in Source pixel coordinates. Nonempty results are zero-based; an empty result has range 1 .. 0.

Hough_Circle_Threshold

subtype Hough_Circle_Threshold is Positive range 1 .. 2_147_483_647;

Integer Canny and accumulator thresholds for gradient Hough circles, 1 through 2_147_483_647: the upper Canny threshold and the accumulator vote threshold. They are integers because OpenCV rounds both to integers.

Hough_Circle_With_Votes

type Hough_Circle_With_Votes is record
   Center : OpenCV.Float32_Point;
   Radius : OpenCV.Float32_Value;
   Votes  : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
end record;

A radius-finding gradient-Hough circle with the support count of its estimated radius, from OpenCV's binary32 Vec4f output (counts above 2**24 may already have been rounded by binary32).

Record fields
Center

Circle center in Source pixel coordinates, quantized to accumulator cells.

Radius

Estimated circle radius in pixels, a histogram estimate.

Votes

Support count of the chosen radius, an integer stored as Float64; ranking evidence, not a cross-version constant.

Hough_Circle_With_Votes_Array

type Hough_Circle_With_Votes_Array is
  array (Natural range <>) of Hough_Circle_With_Votes;

Result of Find_Hough_Circles_With_Votes. Nonempty results are zero-based; an empty result has range 1 .. 0.

Hough_Degree

Hough_Degree : constant := Ada.Numerics.Pi / 180.0;

One degree in radians (Pi / 180), the default point-set angle resolution of Find_Hough_Lines_From_Points.

Hough_Line

type Hough_Line is record
   Rho           : OpenCV.Float32_Value;
   Angle_Radians : OpenCV.Float32_Value;
end record;

A standard-Hough line in polar form: the points (X, Y) satisfying X * cos (Angle_Radians) + Y * sin (Angle_Radians) = Rho, with X the column and Y the row. Each line is an accumulator bin quantized by the requested resolutions. A horizontal line at row r appears near (r, Pi / 2); a vertical line at column c appears near (c, 0), or equivalently (-c, Pi).

Record fields
Rho

Signed distance from the Source origin to the line along its normal, in pixels; may be negative.

Angle_Radians

Direction of the line normal; always in radians.

Hough_Line_Array

type Hough_Line_Array is array (Natural range <>) of Hough_Line;

Result of Find_Hough_Lines. Nonempty results are zero-based; an empty result has range 1 .. 0.

Hough_Line_Segment

type Hough_Line_Segment is record
   Start_Point : OpenCV.Point;
   End_Point   : OpenCV.Point;
end record;

A probabilistic-Hough segment. Endpoint order is unspecified: (A, B) and (B, A) describe the same segment. Endpoints are integer pixel positions and may differ by a pixel or two across OpenCV builds.

Record fields
Start_Point

One endpoint, in Source pixel coordinates.

End_Point

The other endpoint, in Source pixel coordinates.

Hough_Line_Segment_Array

type Hough_Line_Segment_Array is
  array (Natural range <>) of Hough_Line_Segment;

Result of Find_Hough_Line_Segments. Nonempty results are zero-based; an empty result has range 1 .. 0.

Hough_Line_With_Votes

type Hough_Line_With_Votes is record
   Rho           : OpenCV.Float32_Value;
   Angle_Radians : OpenCV.Float32_Value;
   Votes         : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
end record;

A standard-Hough line with its accumulator vote count. Votes is evidence for ranking, not a cross-version constant: raster-line votes come from OpenCV's binary32 Vec3f output and point-set votes from its Vec3d output, all integer accumulator counts promoted to Float64. Raster counts above 2**24 may already have been rounded by binary32. The line is the set of points satisfying X * cos (Angle_Radians) + Y * sin (Angle_Radians) = Rho; OpenCV's point-set order (votes, rho, theta) is normalized to this layout.

Record fields
Rho

Signed distance from the coordinate origin (the Source origin for raster lines) to the line along its normal.

Angle_Radians

Direction of the line normal; always in radians.

Votes

Accumulator support count of the line's bin, an integer stored as Float64.

Hough_Line_With_Votes_Array

type Hough_Line_With_Votes_Array is
  array (Natural range <>) of Hough_Line_With_Votes;

Result of Find_Hough_Lines_With_Votes and Find_Hough_Lines_From_Points. Nonempty results are zero-based; an empty result has range 1 .. 0.

Hough_Point_Array

type Hough_Point_Array is array (Integer range <>) of OpenCV.Float32_Point;

Input points for Find_Hough_Lines_From_Points; any index bounds, including a null range. X and Y are binary32 coordinates in the same frame as the returned Rho; that operation rejects nonfinite values.

Hough_Vote_Threshold

subtype Hough_Vote_Threshold is Positive range 1 .. 2_147_483_647;

Accumulator vote threshold, 1 through 2_147_483_647, of the Hough line, segment and point-set operations and of Find_Ballard_Template_Matches. It is not itself an accepted count: standard lines and Ballard matches need strictly more votes than the threshold. Each operation states how its results relate to it.

Initialize_GrabCut

function Initialize_GrabCut
  (Source       : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Initial_Mask : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Iterations   : GrabCut_Iterations := 1) return GrabCut_State

Initializes from Source and a caller-built label mask. Initial_Mask must be a two-dimensional UInt8 C1 Mat with Source's rows and columns holding only values 0 .. 3 (see GrabCut_Label_Value), with at least GrabCut_Minimum_Training_Pixels background (definite or probable) and foreground (definite or probable) pixels. The state owns a deep copy; Initial_Mask and Source are not modified. Iterations rounds of segmentation then run; definite labels keep their values. Source requirements and size limits are those of the rectangle overload.

Parameters
Source

Nonempty 2-D UInt8 C3 image to segment.

Initial_Mask

Nonempty UInt8 C1 label mask of Source's geometry, values 0 .. 3.

Iterations

Segmentation rounds to run; defaults to 1.

Return Value

New initialized state owning a segmented copy of Initial_Mask and the learned models.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an invalid Source, an Initial_Mask that is empty, not 2-D UInt8 C1, of another geometry or holds a value above 3, fewer than five background or foreground pixels, a native size limit, or an OpenCV failure.

Initialize_GrabCut

function Initialize_GrabCut
  (Source            : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Initial_Mask      : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Foreground_Region : OpenCV.Rect;
   Iterations        : GrabCut_Iterations := 1) return GrabCut_State

Combines caller labels with a rectangle: every label outside the region becomes Definite_Background, while all four labels inside are preserved before segmentation. Requires five pixels of each training class in the constrained mask. Source and Initial_Mask are unchanged. The binding applies the rectangle to a private copy of Initial_Mask and runs native mask initialization, because OpenCV's combined rectangle-or-mask flag selects mask mode alone. Foreground_Region must have positive size and lie inside Source; background labels inside it count towards the five background pixels. Otherwise as the mask overload.

Parameters
Source

Nonempty 2-D UInt8 C3 image to segment.

Initial_Mask

Nonempty UInt8 C1 label mask of Source's geometry, values 0 .. 3; its labels inside Foreground_Region are kept.

Foreground_Region

Rectangle outside which every label becomes Definite_Background.

Iterations

Segmentation rounds to run; defaults to 1.

Return Value

New initialized state owning the constrained, segmented mask and the learned models.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an invalid Source or Initial_Mask, an empty Foreground_Region or one outside Source, fewer than five pixels of either class in the constrained mask, a native size limit, or an OpenCV failure.

Initialize_GrabCut

function Initialize_GrabCut
  (Source            : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Foreground_Region : OpenCV.Rect;
   Iterations        : GrabCut_Iterations := 1) return GrabCut_State

Initializes from Source, a nonempty two-dimensional UInt8 C3 image, and Foreground_Region, which must have positive size, lie entirely inside Source, and leave at least GrabCut_Minimum_Training_Pixels pixels both inside and outside. Pixels outside the region start as Definite_Background and inside as Probable_Foreground; Iterations rounds of segmentation then run. Source is not modified. Foreground_Region coordinates are relative to the supplied Source view. Exact labels near object boundaries can differ between OpenCV 4.1 and later releases, whose mixture initialization differs. The pixel count must fit OpenCV's signed-int graph-size arithmetic.

Parameters
Source

Nonempty 2-D UInt8 C3 image to segment.

Foreground_Region

Rectangle that contains the object; pixels outside it are definite background.

Iterations

Segmentation rounds to run; defaults to 1.

Return Value

New initialized state with a UInt8 C1 label mask of Source's rows and columns and the learned models.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised when Source is not a nonempty 2-D UInt8 C3 image, when Foreground_Region is empty, extends outside Source or leaves fewer than five pixels inside or outside, for a native size limit, or when OpenCV fails.

Integral_Images

function Integral_Images
  (Source        : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Sum_Depth     : Integral_Sum_Depth := Float64_Integral;
   Squared_Depth : Integral_Squared_Depth := Float64_Squared_Integral)
   return Integral_Images_Result

Computes the summed-area table, the squared-sample integral and OpenCV's 45-degree tilted sum of Source in one native call (see Integral_Images_Result). Tilted_Sum has Sum's depth. Source contract, depth pairs, geometry, Region, Int32 overflow and publication rules are those of Integral_Sum_And_Squares; the tilted scratch indexing adds its own native int bound on the channel-expanded width.

Parameters
Source

Nonempty 2-D UInt8, Float32 or Float64 image to integrate.

Sum_Depth

Depth of the Sum and Tilted_Sum components; defaults to Float64_Integral.

Squared_Depth

Depth of the Squared_Sum component; defaults to Float64_Squared_Integral.

Return Value

Record holding the fresh Sum, Squared_Sum and Tilted_Sum Mats.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an invalid Source, an unsupported depth pair, an Int32 total overflow, a native int arithmetic limit, or a native failure.

Integral_Images_Result

type Integral_Images_Result is record
   Sum         : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Squared_Sum : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Tilted_Sum  : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
end record;

Result of Integral_Images: three fresh owning Mats with Source's channel count and (Source.Rows + 1) x (Source.Columns + 1) geometry. Channels are summed independently.

Record fields
Sum

Summed-area table: the element at row Y, column X is the sum of source pixels with row < Y and column < X; the first row and column are zero. Its depth is selected by Sum_Depth.

Squared_Sum

Sum of squared source samples over the same rectangles as Sum, with a zero first row and column. Its depth is selected by Squared_Depth.

Tilted_Sum

OpenCV's 45-degree rotated sum: the element at row Y, column X adds source pixels (x, y) with y < Y and abs (x - X + 1) <= Y - y - 1. It has the same depth as Sum.

Integral_Squared_Depth

type Integral_Squared_Depth is
  (Float32_Squared_Integral, Float64_Squared_Integral);

Depth of the Squared_Sum output of Integral_Sum_And_Squares and Integral_Images.

Enumeration Literal
Float32_Squared_Integral

Float32 squared sums. Rejected together with a Float64_Integral Sum, and therefore for Float64 sources.

Float64_Squared_Integral

Float64 squared sums, accepted with every Sum depth; the default.

Integral_Sum

function Integral_Sum
  (Source    : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Sum_Depth : Integral_Sum_Depth := Float64_Integral)
   return OpenCV.Core.Mat

Computes the summed-area table of Source: the element at row Y, column X is the sum of source pixels with row < Y and column < X. With S (row, column) denoting that element, the rectangle [X1, X2) x [Y1, Y2) totals S (Y2, X2) - S (Y1, X2) - S (Y2, X1) + S (Y1, X1).

Nonempty 2-D UInt8, Float32 or Float64 source with any channel count. Each channel is independent; the result is a fresh (Rows+1, Columns+1) Mat with Source's channel count and a zero top row and left column. A Region is its own logical image: pixels outside it do not participate. Source is unchanged. The sum is computed privately and published only after success. UInt8/Int32 is rejected if any actual channel total exceeds INT_MAX. Floating sums follow the selected precision and IEEE rounding, overflow and NaN propagation; there is no finite-value scan. Geometry, channel-expanded widths and strides that would overflow OpenCV's native int arithmetic are rejected.

Parameters
Source

Nonempty 2-D UInt8, Float32 or Float64 image to integrate.

Sum_Depth

Depth of the result; see Integral_Sum_Depth for the pairings each source depth accepts. Defaults to Float64_Integral.

Return Value

Fresh owning summed-area Mat.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D or unsupported-depth Source, an unsupported Sum_Depth, an Int32 total overflow, a native int arithmetic limit, or a native failure.

Integral_Sum_And_Squares

function Integral_Sum_And_Squares
  (Source        : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Sum_Depth     : Integral_Sum_Depth := Float64_Integral;
   Squared_Depth : Integral_Squared_Depth := Float64_Squared_Integral)
   return Integral_Sum_And_Squares_Result

Computes the summed-area table and the integral of squared samples of Source in one native call (see Integral_Sum_And_Squares_Result). The Source contract, geometry, Region, Int32 overflow and native limit rules are those of Integral_Sum. Both Mats are fresh owning storage published only after complete success. Accepted (Sum_Depth, Squared_Depth) pairs: UInt8 accepts all except (Float64_Integral, Float32_Squared_Integral); Float32 accepts (Float32, Float32), (Float32, Float64) and (Float64, Float64); Float64 accepts only (Float64, Float64).

Parameters
Source

Nonempty 2-D UInt8, Float32 or Float64 image to integrate.

Sum_Depth

Depth of the Sum component; defaults to Float64_Integral.

Squared_Depth

Depth of the Squared_Sum component; defaults to Float64_Squared_Integral.

Return Value

Record holding the fresh Sum and Squared_Sum Mats.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an invalid Source, an unsupported depth pair, an Int32 total overflow, a native int arithmetic limit, or a native failure.

Integral_Sum_And_Squares_Result

type Integral_Sum_And_Squares_Result is record
   Sum         : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Squared_Sum : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
end record;

Result of Integral_Sum_And_Squares: two fresh owning Mats with Source's channel count and (Source.Rows + 1) x (Source.Columns + 1) geometry, each with a zero first row and first column. Channels are summed independently.

Record fields
Sum

Summed-area table: the element at row Y, column X is the sum of source pixels with row < Y and column < X. Its depth is selected by Sum_Depth.

Squared_Sum

Sum of squared source samples over the same rectangles as Sum. Its depth is selected by Squared_Depth.

Integral_Sum_Depth

type Integral_Sum_Depth is
  (Int32_Integral, Float32_Integral, Float64_Integral);

Depth of the summed-area (Sum) and tilted-sum outputs of Integral_Sum, Integral_Sum_And_Squares and Integral_Images. UInt8 sources accept every literal, Float32 sources Float32_Integral or Float64_Integral, and Float64 sources only Float64_Integral; other pairings raise OpenCV_Error.

Enumeration Literal

Int32 sums, for UInt8 sources only. Rejected when the actual total of any channel exceeds 2_147_483_647.

Float32_Integral

Float32 sums, for UInt8 or Float32 sources, with Float32 rounding.

Float64_Integral

Float64 sums, for every supported source depth; the default of every integral function.

Interpolation_Method

type Interpolation_Method is
  (Nearest_Neighbor, Linear, Cubic, Area, Lanczos_4, Linear_Exact);

Resampling interpolation, passed to OpenCV as the native INTER_* flag named below. Each operation accepts a subset and rejects the rest with OpenCV_Error: Resize accepts all six (Linear_Exact only for UInt8, UInt16 and Int16 sources); Warp_Affine and Warp_Perspective accept Nearest_Neighbor and Linear; Warp_Polar and every Remap overload accept Nearest_Neighbor, Linear, Cubic and Lanczos_4.

Area

Pixel-area resampling (INTER_AREA); Resize only.

Lanczos_4

Lanczos interpolation (INTER_LANCZOS4).

Linear_Exact

Native fixed-point bilinear interpolation with deterministic integer rounding (INTER_LINEAR_EXACT); Resize only, for UInt8, UInt16 and Int16 sources.

Laplacian

procedure Laplacian
  (Source            : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination       : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination_Depth : Derivative_Depth := Float32_Depth;
   Kernel_Size       : Laplacian_Kernel_Size := 1;
   Scale             : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 1.0;
   Offset            : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 0.0;
   Border            : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Reflect_101)

Laplacian computes the sum of the second X and Y derivatives for every Source channel (native cv::Laplacian). Source must be non-empty and two-dimensional with depth UInt8, UInt16, Int16, Float32, or Float64. Destination_Depth follows the same source/destination combinations as Sobel. Kernel size 1 uses OpenCV's dedicated 3 by 3 Laplacian aperture. Scale multiplies computed values and Offset is added before storage. Channels are processed independently.

Destination receives Source's rows, columns, channels, and requested depth as a direct native output: storage that already has that size and type is reused (shallow copies and a Region's parent see the result); otherwise Destination is rebound to new storage. Source remains unchanged when distinct from Destination. Direct in-place operation is supported when Destination_Depth is Same_Depth.

Parameters
Source

Image to differentiate.

Destination

Receives the scaled Laplacian image.

Destination_Depth

Output depth; Float32_Depth by default (see Derivative_Depth for the supported combinations).

Kernel_Size

Aperture size; must be odd and between 1 and 31.

Scale

Factor applied to each computed value; must be finite.

Offset

Value added after scaling (OpenCV's delta); must be finite.

Border

Extrapolation outside Source: Constant_Border, Replicate, Reflect, or Reflect_101 (default). Wrap is rejected.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D, or unsupported Source, an unsupported depth combination, an even Kernel_Size, a nonfinite Scale or Offset, Wrap, or a failure reported by OpenCV.

Laplacian_Kernel_Size

type Laplacian_Kernel_Size is range 1 .. 31;

Aperture size of Laplacian, in pixels. Laplacian accepts only odd values: 1 selects OpenCV's dedicated 3 x 3 Laplacian aperture and 3 through 31 select that aperture size.

Laplacian_Precision

type Laplacian_Precision is
  (Automatic_Precision, Float32_Precision, Float64_Precision);

Working floating depth of a Laplacian pyramid. Residuals are never computed in an integer type, so negative detail coefficients never saturate.

Enumeration Literal
Automatic_Precision

Keeps Float64 sources in Float64 and promotes every other supported depth to Float32.

Float32_Precision

Always Float32, narrowing a Float64 Source.

Float64_Precision

Always Float64, for every supported depth.

Manhattan_Distance_Transform_UInt8

function Manhattan_Distance_Transform_UInt8
  (Source : OpenCV.Core.Mat) return OpenCV.Core.Mat

Exact L1 distance, UInt8 C1, saturating at 255. Each pixel receives |dx| + |dy| to its nearest zero pixel. Source requirements, Region behavior and native size limits are those of Distance_Transform with Manhattan_Distance. There is no UInt8 Euclidean variant.

Parameters
Source

Nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1 mask containing at least one zero pixel; zero marks the targets.

Return Value

Fresh owning UInt8 C1 distance Mat with Source's rows and columns; distances above 255 are stored as 255.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised when Source is empty, not 2-D, not UInt8 C1 or has no zero pixel, when a native size limit is exceeded, or when OpenCV fails.

Match_Template

procedure Match_Template
  (Source      : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Template    : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Mask        : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Method      : Template_Matching_Method := Squared_Difference)

Masked Match_Template scores only the Template pixels that Mask includes, otherwise sliding Template over Source like the unmasked overload. Masked matching accepts nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1 inputs (Source, Template and Mask). Mask must match Template dimensions and Template must fit Source. A zero mask byte excludes a pixel; every nonzero mask byte (1, 127 and 255 alike) includes a pixel with weight one. Supported methods are exactly Squared_Difference (the default), Normalized_Squared_Difference, Cross_Correlation, Normalized_Cross_Correlation and Correlation_Coefficient; Normalized_Correlation_Coefficient (TM_CCOEFF_NORMED) is not supported and raises OpenCV_Error. Intensities stay in original UInt8 units (0 .. 255); the binding passes owning unscaled Float32 copies and an exact 0/1 Float32 mask, avoiding OpenCV 4.1's UInt8 scaling by 1/255.

Private logical snapshots isolate Regions and shared storage: only logical pixels are read, parent pixels never participate, and inputs are unchanged. Destination receives fresh owning Float32 C1 storage only on success, sized Source minus Template plus one (Source.Rows - Template.Rows + 1 rows by Source.Columns - Template.Columns + 1 columns); on failure it is unchanged, and its old storage is never reused. Distinct Mat headers that share pixel storage with an input are supported, but the same Mat object as Destination and as an input is rejected. Snapshot cost is linear in input pixels. Requests whose native correlation arithmetic or byte extents cannot be represented are rejected before native execution; this includes a Template area above INT_MAX / 4 for Cross_Correlation and for the OpenCV 4.1 compositions below.

Squared_Difference scores are unnormalized sums of squared differences (lower is better); an all-zero mask yields zero scores.

Cross_Correlation is the unnormalized sum of included intensity products in original UInt8 units: choose the maximum-scoring location. It supports black Templates and all-zero masks, with zero correlation. Native unmasked correlation uses an owning zeroed Float32 Template on every supported OpenCV version. Scores retain native rounding.

Normalized_Cross_Correlation scores are native cosine similarities (higher is better) and require at least one pixel where both Mask and Template are nonzero. Ideal defined scores lie in [0, 1]; native rounding is retained, including values slightly outside that interval. A Scene (Source) window whose included samples are all zero receives exact Float32 +0.0 through a binding-owned compatibility rule. Positive-energy windows preserve native scores without clipping or nonfinite repair.

Normalized_Squared_Difference is the included squared error divided by sqrt (template energy * scene energy). Lower is better, and a perfect match is zero. Scores are not clamped to [0, 1]. The method requires positive masked Template energy, at least one pixel where both Mask and Template are nonzero; an all-zero mask is rejected. A Scene window whose included samples are all zero receives exact Float32'Last (IEEE 16#7F7F_FFFF#), a finite worst-match sentinel. That is not the NCC +0.0 rule: zero would rank the undefined window as a perfect match. OpenCV 4.10 and 5 call native masked TM_SQDIFF_NORMED. OpenCV 4.1 composes native masked SQDIFF, native Scene-energy correlation, and native normalization. Positive-energy scores are not repaired; a nonfinite one raises OpenCV_Error instead of being published.

The zero-window check of both normalized methods adds packed Scene support and one output-row bitset; work is proportional to output rows * active mask pixels * output columns / 64.

Correlation_Coefficient is the unnormalized masked covariance numerator R = Sum(M*T*I) - Sum(M*T)*Sum(M*I)/Sum(M): both means are taken over included pixels only. It is mean-centered but not scale-normalized, so it is not a Pearson coefficient and is not limited to [-1, 1]. Higher is better. Scores may be positive, zero or negative (an inverted pattern scores negative), and a score near zero is not a landmark. Adding a constant offset to Source or Template leaves the ideal score unchanged; Float32 cancellation leaves a small error, so compare with a tolerance that scales with the correlation magnitude, not exactly. Multiplying intensities by a positive factor scales the score. A constant included Template, a constant Scene window, and a single included pixel all have ideal score zero and are accepted. An all-zero mask has an undefined mean and is rejected. OpenCV 4.10 and 5 call native masked TM_CCOEFF. OpenCV 4.1 composes the same result from native unmasked correlation of the centered Template and of the mask, including the native residual-mean correction. Scores retain native rounding without clamping. Example:

Match_Template
  (Scene, Patch, Mask, Scores, Correlation_Coefficient);
Best := OpenCV.Core.Min_Max_Loc (Scores).Maximum_Location;
--  Best is the best match; an offset Scene still finds it.

TM_CCOEFF_NORMED and contract violations raise OpenCV_Error.

Parameters
Source

Image searched (the Scene): nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1.

Template

Patch compared at every placement: nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1, no larger than Source in either dimension.

Mask

Pixel selector with Template's dimensions: nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1, zero excludes and nonzero includes with weight one.

Destination

Rebound to the fresh Float32 C1 score map only on success; unchanged on failure.

Method

One of the five supported methods; default Squared_Difference.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D or non-UInt8 C1 input, a Mask whose dimensions differ from Template's, a Template larger than Source, Normalized_Correlation_Coefficient, no pixel with nonzero Mask and Template (normalized methods), an all-zero Mask (Correlation_Coefficient), Destination being the same Mat as an input, unrepresentable native arithmetic, a nonfinite normalized squared-difference score, or an OpenCV failure.

Match_Template

procedure Match_Template
  (Source      : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Template    : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Method      : Template_Matching_Method)

Match_Template slides Template over Source and writes a score map to Destination: the score at (X, Y) compares Template with the Source window whose top-left pixel is (X, Y). Source and Template must each be a non-empty two-dimensional Mat of depth UInt8 or Float32 with 1 to 4 channels, and they must share the same depth and channel count. Template must fit entirely inside Source: Template.Rows <= Source.Rows and Template.Columns <= Source.Columns. A Template equal in size to Source is valid. Multi-channel inputs are combined into one score per location; they are not converted to grayscale. For Squared_Difference and Normalized_Squared_Difference the best match is the minimum score; for the other four methods the best match is the maximum. Use OpenCV.Core.Min_Max_Loc on Destination to recover those extrema; the extremum location is the best match's top-left pixel in Source.

Destination receives a Float32 C1 Mat of Rows = Source.Rows - Template.Rows + 1 and Columns = Source.Columns - Template.Columns + 1 as direct native output: OpenCV reuses Destination's storage when it already has that size and type (shallow copies of Destination and a Region's parent then see the result) and otherwise rebinds Destination to new storage, so callers need not preallocate it. Source and Template are read-only and may share storage, including the same Mat or a Template that is a Region of Source. Destination must not share storage with Source or Template: the same Mat, or a Destination whose data starts at Source's or Template's data, is rejected; other overlaps are not detected. Inputs are validated before OpenCV runs. Contract violations and failures reported by OpenCV raise OpenCV.OpenCV_Error.

Parameters
Source

Image searched: nonempty 2-D UInt8 or Float32 with 1 to 4 channels.

Template

Patch compared at every placement: same depth and channel count as Source, and no larger than Source in either dimension.

Destination

Receives the Float32 C1 score map by direct native output; must not share storage with Source or Template.

Method

Comparison to compute, with no default; see Template_Matching_Method for score meanings and best-match extrema.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty or non-2-D Source or Template, an unsupported depth or channel count, mismatched Source and Template types, a Template larger than Source, a Destination sharing Source or Template storage, or an OpenCV failure.

Maximum_Histogram_Dimensions

Maximum_Histogram_Dimensions : constant Positive := 10;

Maximum histogram dimensionality guaranteed on every supported OpenCV generation. OpenCV 4.x allows dense Mats of up to CV_MAX_DIM (32) dimensions, but OpenCV 5.0 limits Mat to MatShape::MAX_DIMS (10), and a dense histogram is stored in one N-dimensional Mat.

Maximum_Pyramid_Level_Count

function Maximum_Pyramid_Level_Count
  (Source : OpenCV.Core.Mat) return Positive

Maximum_Pyramid_Level_Count returns the number of distinct natural Gaussian levels of Source, including level 0: both extents are halved as (N + 1) / 2 until they both reach 1. For example 1 x 1 -> 1, 8 x 8 -> 4, and 9 x 7 -> 5. Only the geometry is inspected; depth and channel count are not checked. The result is the largest Level_Count accepted by Build_Gaussian_Pyramid and Build_Laplacian_Pyramid.

Parameters
Source

Non-empty two-dimensional Mat whose rows and columns are measured.

Return Value

Number of distinct natural levels; at least 1.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised when Source is empty or not two-dimensional.

Mean_Shift_Iteration_Limit

subtype Mean_Shift_Iteration_Limit is Positive range 1 .. 100;

Maximum number of mean-shift iterations per pixel, 1 through 100; the type of Mean_Shift_Termination.Maximum_Iterations.

Mean_Shift_Pyramid_Level

subtype Mean_Shift_Pyramid_Level is Natural range 0 .. 8;

Highest Gaussian pyramid level processed by Pyramid_Mean_Shift_Filter; 0 filters Source directly without a pyramid. A level N above zero builds N further natural half-size levels and starts filtering at the top with Spatial_Radius / 2**N.

Mean_Shift_Termination

type Mean_Shift_Termination is record
   Maximum_Iterations : Mean_Shift_Iteration_Limit := 5;
   Epsilon            : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 1.0;
end record;

Per-pixel mean-shift stopping rule. Iteration stops after Maximum_Iterations or once the combined spatial/color shift is at most Epsilon. Both criteria are always active (native MAX_ITER and EPS). Epsilon must be finite and nonnegative; Pyramid_Mean_Shift_Filter checks it. The defaults, 5 iterations and 1.0, match OpenCV's own.

Record fields
Maximum_Iterations

Iteration limit for each pixel's mean-shift (1 through 100; default 5).

Epsilon

Shift threshold (default 1.0). The native shift measure is the absolute X plus absolute Y displacement in pixels plus the squared RGB color change of one iteration.

Measure_Text

function Measure_Text
  (Text       : String;
   Font       : Text_Font := Hershey_Simplex;
   Font_Scale : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 1.0;
   Thickness  : Drawing_Thickness := 1) return Text_Metrics

Measures the rendered extent of Text with cv::getTextSize, without drawing. Empty text returns Size (0, 0) and Baseline 0 without calling OpenCV; Font_Scale is still validated. Otherwise the result is the native bounding Size and the unadjusted native baseline, which is the distance from the bottom-most text point up to the baseline. Metrics depend on the OpenCV generation (see Text_Font). Nonempty text is subject to the Draw_Text Font_Scale and text-length preflight.

Parameters
Text

Bytes to measure, passed with their exact length, including embedded NULs and any lower bound.

Font

Font selector; defaults to Hershey_Simplex.

Font_Scale

Font scale factor: finite, positive and within the Draw_Text preflight bound. Defaults to 1.0.

Thickness

Glyph stroke thickness; defaults to 1.

Return Value

The text's bounding Size and Baseline, in pixels.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised when Font_Scale is not finite and positive, when nonempty text fails the preflight, and when OpenCV fails.

Median_Blur

procedure Median_Blur
  (Source      : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Kernel_Size : Median_Kernel_Size)

Median_Blur replaces each Source pixel with the median of its square Kernel_Size neighborhood (native cv::medianBlur). Source must be a non-empty two-dimensional Mat with 1, 3, or 4 channels. Kernel sizes 3 and 5 accept UInt8, UInt16, or Float32; larger kernels accept UInt8 only. Channels are filtered independently. OpenCV uses replicated-border handling internally; no public Border parameter is exposed.

Destination receives Source's rows, columns, depth, and channel count as a direct native output: storage that already has that size and type is reused (shallow copies and a Region's parent see the result); otherwise Destination is rebound to new storage. Direct in-place operation (Destination is Source) is supported; Source remains unchanged when distinct from Destination.

Parameters
Source

Image to filter.

Destination

Receives the median-filtered image.

Kernel_Size

Side length of the square aperture in pixels; must be odd (the subtype excludes values below 3).

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty or non-2-D Source, a channel count other than 1, 3, or 4, an even Kernel_Size, a depth not accepted for Kernel_Size, or a failure reported by OpenCV.

Median_Kernel_Size

subtype Median_Kernel_Size is Positive range 3 .. 2_147_483_647;

Side length, in pixels, of Median_Blur's square aperture. The subtype excludes values below 3; Median_Blur also requires an odd value.

Morphology_Border_Value

type Morphology_Border_Value is private;

Constant border value used by morphology when Border is Constant_Border: either OpenCV's neutral default (Default_Morphology_Border) or an explicit Scalar created by Explicit_Morphology_Border. Other borders ignore it.

Morphology_Iterations

subtype Morphology_Iterations is Positive range 1 .. 2_147_483_647;

Repetition count of morphology. Erode and Dilate apply the elementary step Iterations times. Apply_Morphology repeats each constituent step: Opening with 2 performs two erosions and then two dilations, not two complete openings. Hit_Or_Miss erodes each leg Iterations times.

Morphology_Operation

type Morphology_Operation is
  (Opening, Closing, Gradient, Top_Hat, Black_Hat);

Compound operation of Apply_Morphology, built from erosion and dilation with the same structuring element.

Enumeration Literal
Opening

Erosion followed by dilation; removes small bright features.

Closing

Dilation followed by erosion; fills small dark holes.

Gradient

Dilation minus erosion, a morphological edge map.

Top_Hat

Source minus its opening; keeps small bright features.

Black_Hat

Closing of Source minus Source; keeps small dark features.

Morphology_Shape

type Morphology_Shape is (Rectangle, Cross, Ellipse);

Shape of a generated structuring element, built by Get_Structuring_Element and by the Kernel_Size morphology overloads. Any 1 x 1 element is a single included pixel whatever the shape.

Enumeration Literal
Rectangle

Every pixel of the Kernel_Size box is included.

Cross

Only the anchor's row and column are included; the centered anchor is (Width / 2, Height / 2).

Ellipse

An elliptical footprint fitted to the box, symmetric about its center. Native signed radius arithmetic limits the height to 92681.

Nonuniform_Histogram_Dimension

function Nonuniform_Histogram_Dimension
  (Channel : Natural; Boundaries : Histogram_Bin_Boundary_Array)
   return Histogram_Nonuniform_Dimension

Returns a single-source nonuniform axis (source position 0) that counts Channel of the Source into the bins defined by Boundaries: N edges define N - 1 bins [Edges (I), Edges (I + 1)). The descriptor owns a copy of the edges. Channel is checked against the Source when the histogram is calculated.

Parameters
Channel

Zero-based channel of the Source sampled on this axis.

Boundaries

At least two finite, strictly increasing edges in iteration order; the array's index bounds are irrelevant.

Return Value

Axis descriptor with Boundaries'Length - 1 bins.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for fewer than two edges, a nonfinite edge, or edges that are not strictly increasing.

Nonuniform_Histogram_Source_Dimension

function Nonuniform_Histogram_Source_Dimension

   Channel         : Natural;
   Boundaries      : Histogram_Bin_Boundary_Array)
   return Histogram_Nonuniform_Dimension

Returns a nonuniform axis of a multi-source histogram that counts Channel of the source at Source_Position into the bins defined by Boundaries, as for Nonuniform_Histogram_Dimension. Source_Position and Channel are checked against the sources when the histogram is calculated; single-source operations require position 0.

Parameters
Source_Position

Zero-based position in Mat_Array iteration order; position 0 is Sources'First, whatever the array's bounds.

Channel

Zero-based channel within the selected source.

Boundaries

At least two finite, strictly increasing edges in iteration order; the array's index bounds are irrelevant.

Return Value

Axis descriptor with Boundaries'Length - 1 bins.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Optional_Contour_Index

type Optional_Contour_Index (Present : Boolean := False) is record
   case Present is
      when False =>
         null;
      when True =>
         Index : Contour_Index;
   end case;
end record;

A contour hierarchy link that may be absent, replacing OpenCV's -1 sentinel. The default value is absent.

Record fields
Present

True when the link designates a contour; False when OpenCV reported no such contour (a negative native index).

Index
