Phase_Correlate

function Phase_Correlate
  (Source_1, Source_2 : OpenCV.Core.Mat) return Phase_Correlation_Result

Estimates the translation between two images of identical type and geometry with native cv::phaseCorrelate, without a window. Shift describes Source_2 relative to Source_1: right/down is positive. Apply the negative shift to Source_2 to align it with Source_1.

Sources must be nonempty 2-D Float32 or Float64 C1 Mats with identical depth, rows and columns. No conversion, normalization or finiteness scan is performed; nonfinite samples retain native behavior and can give numerically unhelpful results. Both sources are independently snapshotted into packed private copies before native execution: Regions contribute only logical pixels, the same or overlapping storage may be passed twice, and inputs are unchanged on success or failure. Concurrent external mutation is unsupported. The native transform pads to optimal DFT sizes; unsupported sizes and signed-arithmetic or allocation limits of the padded plane raise OpenCV_Error. Response is not promised to match across releases: OpenCV 4.1 squares special DC/Nyquist spectrum entries where 4.10 and 5.0 use their absolute value.

Parameters
Source_1

Reference image.

Source_2

Image whose displacement relative to Source_1 is measured.

Return Value

Shift of Source_2 and peak Response.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Empty, non-2-D, non-floating or multichannel source, mismatched sources, native DFT size limits, or an OpenCV failure.

Phase_Correlate

function Phase_Correlate
  (Source_1, Source_2, Window : OpenCV.Core.Mat)
   return Phase_Correlation_Result

Windowed Phase_Correlate: Window multiplies both sources before the native DFT (cv::phaseCorrelate window argument). Window must be a nonempty 2-D C1 Mat with the sources' depth, rows and columns, for example from Create_Hanning_Window; an empty Window is rejected. Window is snapshotted like the sources, so it may share storage with either source and is never modified. Source requirements, the right/down-positive shift of Source_2 relative to Source_1, Response, Region, alias and limit rules are those of the two-source overload.

Parameters
Source_1

Reference image.

Source_2

Image whose displacement relative to Source_1 is measured.

Window

Weighting window applied to both sources.

Return Value

Shift of Source_2 and peak Response.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Invalid or mismatched sources, empty or mismatched Window, native DFT size limits, or an OpenCV failure.

Phase_Correlation_Result

type Phase_Correlation_Result is record
   X_Shift, Y_Shift, Response : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
end record;

Translation estimated by Phase_Correlate: the native subpixel displacement of Source_2 relative to Source_1, in pixels, and the strength of the correlation peak. Right/down is positive.

Record fields
X_Shift

Column (X) displacement of Source_2 relative to Source_1; positive when Source_2 content lies to the right.

Y_Shift

Row (Y) displacement of Source_2 relative to Source_1; positive when Source_2 content lies lower.

Response

Native normalized 5x5 peak energy: the energy of the weighted peak centroid divided by the padded DFT plane size. It indicates peak concentration; it is not a probability or a guaranteed [0, 1] value, and rounding can exceed the ideal bounds.

Pixel_Connectivity

type Pixel_Connectivity is (Four_Connected, Eight_Connected);

Pixel neighborhood that defines connectivity for connected-component labeling and flood fill. Unrelated to Drawing_Line_Style.

Enumeration Literal
Four_Connected

Pixels connect through their four horizontal and vertical neighbors only, so diagonal pixels stay separate.

Eight_Connected

Pixels also connect through their four diagonal neighbors.

Polar_Direction

type Polar_Direction is (Cartesian_To_Polar, Polar_To_Cartesian);

Direction of a Warp_Polar transform.

Enumeration Literal
Cartesian_To_Polar

Forward transform: Source is a Cartesian image and Destination the polar image (X = rho, Y = angle).

Polar_To_Cartesian

Inverse transform (native WARP_INVERSE_MAP): Source is a polar image and Destination its Cartesian reconstruction.

Polar_Mapping

type Polar_Mapping is (Linear_Polar, Logarithmic_Polar);

Radial scale of a Warp_Polar polar image, whose X coordinate is the radius rho.

Enumeration Literal
Linear_Polar

Rho is proportional to the distance from Center (native WARP_POLAR_LINEAR).

Logarithmic_Polar

Rho follows log(distance + 1) (native WARP_POLAR_LOG); requires Maximum_Radius > 1.

Pre_Corner_Response

function Pre_Corner_Response
  (Source   : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Aperture : Corner_Aperture := Aperture_3;
   Border   : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Reflect_101)
   return OpenCV.Core.Mat

Pre-corner feature response map (native cv::preCornerDetect), built from Sobel derivatives up to second order; it has no block size and is zero on flat image areas. Source requirements, snapshot and Region isolation, border support and native limits are those of Corner_Minimum_Eigenvalue.

Parameters
Source

Nonempty 2-D UInt8 or Float32 C1 image to analyse.

Aperture

Sobel derivative aperture; default Aperture_3.

Border

Constant_Border, Replicate, Reflect or Reflect_101 (the default); Wrap is rejected.

Return Value

Fresh owning Float32 C1 Mat with Source's rows and columns.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Unsupported Source type or layout, Wrap border, native size limits, or an OpenCV failure.

Pyramid_Down

procedure Pyramid_Down
  (Source      : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Border      : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Reflect_101)

Pyramid_Down performs OpenCV's Gaussian-pyramid downsampling of Source (native cv::pyrDown). Source must be a non-empty two-dimensional Mat with depth UInt8, UInt16, Int16, Float32, or Float64. Channel count is unrestricted. Destination receives Source's depth and channel count with OpenCV's natural size: Columns = (Source.Columns + 1) / 2 and Rows = (Source.Rows + 1) / 2. A Region is processed as its own logical image (OpenCV receives a private copy); parent pixels outside the Region do not participate.

Destination is a direct native output: storage that already has the result's size and type is reused (shallow copies and a Region's parent see the result); otherwise Destination is rebound to new storage. Direct in-place operation is not supported: a Destination that is Source or shares or overlaps its storage, including overlapping Regions, is rejected. Extreme geometries are rejected before native signed-int pyramid arithmetic can overflow.

Parameters
Source

Image to downsample.

Destination

Receives the half-size image.

Border

Extrapolation outside Source: Replicate, Reflect, Reflect_101 (default), or Wrap. Constant_Border is rejected.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D, or unsupported-depth Source, Constant_Border, aliased or overlapping Source and Destination storage, an unsafe geometry, or a failure reported by OpenCV.

Pyramid_Mean_Shift_Filter

procedure Pyramid_Mean_Shift_Filter
  (Source                : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination           : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Spatial_Radius        : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Color_Radius          : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Maximum_Pyramid_Level : Mean_Shift_Pyramid_Level := 1;
   Termination           : Mean_Shift_Termination :=
     (Maximum_Iterations => 5, Epsilon => 1.0))

Pyramid_Mean_Shift_Filter performs OpenCV's mean-shift filtering (posterization) stage of mean-shift segmentation (native cv::pyrMeanShiftFiltering). Each pixel is replaced by the color at which a joint spatial/color mean-shift converges: pixels within Spatial_Radius in space and Color_Radius in RGB distance are averaged repeatedly. The result is a filtered color image; it does not produce connected-region labels.

Source must be a non-empty two-dimensional UInt8 C3 Mat. With Maximum_Pyramid_Level > 0, OpenCV first filters a natural Gaussian pyramid from its top level and propagates results downward, halving Spatial_Radius per level; results may differ from level-0 filtering. Every generated level must stay at least 2 x 2: requests whose top level would be smaller are rejected (never silently reduced) because OpenCV's propagation performs invalid pointer arithmetic on such tiny levels.

Source is snapshotted into a private packed copy, so a Region is its own logical image and parent pixels never participate; Source is not modified. The result is computed into fresh storage and bound to Destination only on success; on failure Destination is unchanged. Destination may be Source itself or share its storage. Requests whose native signed-int radius rounding, row offsets, window accumulators, or stopping expression could overflow are rejected before native execution.

Parameters
Source

UInt8 C3 image to filter.

Destination

Rebound on success to a fresh UInt8 C3 image with Source's rows and columns.

Spatial_Radius

Spatial window radius in pixels at level 0; must be finite and positive (OpenCV raises each level's effective radius to at least 1).

Color_Radius

Color window radius as a Euclidean RGB distance; must be finite and nonnegative, and 0 admits only identical colors.

Maximum_Pyramid_Level

Highest pyramid level processed, 1 by default; 0 filters Source directly without a pyramid.

Termination

Per-pixel iteration limit and shift threshold; Termination.Epsilon must be finite and nonnegative.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D, or non-UInt8 C3 Source, an invalid radius or Epsilon, a generated level smaller than 2 x 2, a native arithmetic overflow risk, or a failure reported by OpenCV.

Pyramid_Up

procedure Pyramid_Up
  (Source : OpenCV.Core.Mat; Destination : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat)

Pyramid_Up performs OpenCV's Gaussian-pyramid upsampling of Source (native cv::pyrUp). Source must be a non-empty two-dimensional Mat with depth UInt8, UInt16, Int16, Float32, or Float64. Channel count is unrestricted. Destination receives Source's depth and channel count with exactly doubled rows and columns. A Region is processed as its own logical image, without parent pixels. OpenCV supports only its default border (Reflect_101) for this operation, so no Border parameter is exposed. Pyramid_Up after Pyramid_Down is generally a smoothed reconstruction, not the original image.

Destination is a direct native output: storage that already has the result's size and type is reused (shallow copies and a Region's parent see the result); otherwise Destination is rebound to new storage. Direct in-place operation is not supported: a Destination that is Source or shares or overlaps its storage, including overlapping Regions, is rejected. Extreme geometries are rejected before native signed-int pyramid arithmetic can overflow.

Parameters
Source

Image to upsample.

Destination

Receives the double-size image.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D, or unsupported-depth Source, aliased or overlapping Source and Destination storage, an unsafe geometry, or a failure reported by OpenCV.

Pyramid_Up

procedure Pyramid_Up
  (Source      : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Output_Size : OpenCV.Size)

Pyramid_Up with an explicit Output_Size performs the same Gaussian upsampling (native cv::pyrUp, default border) to the requested size. Each extent must equal twice the Source extent or one less than that (for example 5 -> 9 or 5 -> 10), so every natural Pyramid_Down geometry, including odd sizes, can be reversed exactly. Other sizes, including OpenCV's nonportable twice-plus-one extent, are rejected before any native allocation. The Source contract matches the natural overload. The result is computed into fresh storage and bound to Destination only on success; on failure Destination is unchanged. Destination may therefore share Source storage.

Parameters
Source

Image to upsample.

Destination

Rebound on success to the upsampled image, with Source's depth and channel count.

Output_Size

Requested Width (columns) and Height (rows); each is 2 * N or 2 * N - 1 for the corresponding Source extent N.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an invalid Source, an Output_Size extent other than 2 * N or 2 * N - 1, an unsafe geometry, or a failure reported by OpenCV.

Reconstruct_Laplacian_Pyramid

function Reconstruct_Laplacian_Pyramid
  (Pyramid : OpenCV.Core.Mat_Array) return OpenCV.Core.Mat

Reconstruct_Laplacian_Pyramid inverts Build_Laplacian_Pyramid. Starting from a copy of the last level, each step towards the first level computes

Current := Pyramid_Up (Current, size of Level) + Level

An unmodified pyramid reconstructs the floating working copy of its Source to normal floating-point tolerance, including odd sizes. The result is an independent Mat of the pyramid's floating depth; it is not narrowed to any original integer depth (use Convert_To explicitly). Input levels are never modified.

Parameters
Pyramid

Levels read in array iteration order as fine-to-coarse levels, with any index bounds. It must be non-empty; every level must be a non-empty two-dimensional Mat of one common depth (Float32 or Float64) and one common channel count, and each following level must have Rows = (Previous.Rows + 1) / 2 and Columns = (Previous.Columns + 1) / 2. The whole array is validated before any work begins.

Return Value

Fresh reconstructed image with the first level's geometry, depth and channel count.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised when Pyramid violates its contract or OpenCV reports a failure.

Refine_Corners_Subpixel

function Refine_Corners_Subpixel
  (Source        : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Corners       : Corner_Point_Array;
   Search_Window : OpenCV.Size;
   Dead_Zone     : Corner_Dead_Zone := (Enabled => False);
   Termination   : Corner_Termination :=
     (Maximum_Iterations => 30, Epsilon => 0.01)) return Corner_Point_Array

Refines approximate corner positions to subpixel accuracy with native cv::cornerSubPix and returns the refined points in a new array with the bounds of Corners; Corners is never modified.

Source must be a nonempty 2-D UInt8 or Float32 C1 Mat. Every sample of a Float32 Source must be finite: the whole logical image is scanned, even when Corners is empty; UInt8 sources need no scan. Source is copied into a packed private snapshot, so it is unchanged even on failure and Region neighborhoods never see parent pixels. Every initial point must be finite with 0 <= X < Columns and 0 <= Y < Rows. An empty Corners returns an empty array with the same bounds after the source, window, dead-zone and termination checks. Native signed-int limits on Source size and window scratch raise OpenCV_Error. If the native covariance degenerates, OpenCV 4.1 can return a NaN point where 4.10 and 5.0 keep the previous finite estimate.

Parameters
Source

Image containing the corners.

Corners

Initial estimates; any Integer bounds and at most 2**31 - 1 points.

Search_Window

Search half-size: Width columns and Height rows, both positive. The native search window spans 2 * Width + 1 by 2 * Height + 1 pixels, and Source must have at least 2 * Width + 5 columns and 2 * Height + 5 rows.

Dead_Zone

Optional central dead zone, disabled by default; enabled half-sizes must be smaller than those of Search_Window.

Termination

Iteration limit and Epsilon (finite and nonnegative); default 30 iterations and 0.01.

Return Value

Refined Float32 points, index for index, with the bounds of Corners.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Invalid Source, nonfinite Float32 sample, invalid Search_Window, Dead_Zone, Epsilon or initial point, native layout limits, or an OpenCV failure.

Refine_GrabCut

procedure Refine_GrabCut
  (Source     : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   State      : in out GrabCut_State;
   Iterations : GrabCut_Iterations := 1)

Runs Iterations further rounds that relearn the colour models and re-segment the probable pixels. Source must be a UInt8 C3 image with the state's geometry. Definite labels never change. Source is not modified. The work is done on private copies committed only after success, so a failed refinement leaves State unchanged. Imported models are validated again before native evaluation.

Parameters
Source

Nonempty 2-D UInt8 C3 image with the rows and columns of State's mask.

State

Initialized state, updated in place.

Iterations

Refinement rounds to run; defaults to 1.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised when State is not initialized, when Source is invalid or of another geometry, for an invalid model, a native size limit, or an OpenCV failure.

Refine_GrabCut_Frozen_Model

procedure Refine_GrabCut_Frozen_Model
  (Source : OpenCV.Core.Mat; State : in out GrabCut_State)

Re-segments the probable pixels once with the current colour models held fixed. Otherwise as Refine_GrabCut: the models are not changed, Source must match the state's geometry, definite labels never change, and a failure leaves State unchanged.

Parameters
Source

Nonempty 2-D UInt8 C3 image with the rows and columns of State's mask.

State

Initialized state whose mask is updated in place.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised when State is not initialized, when Source is invalid or of another geometry, for an invalid model, a native size limit, or an OpenCV failure.

Remap

procedure Remap
  (Source        : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Map_X         : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Map_Y         : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination   : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Interpolation : Interpolation_Method := Linear;
   Border        : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Constant_Border;
   Border_Value  : OpenCV.Scalar := (others => 0.0))

Remap applies an absolute source-coordinate map with native cv::remap. Destination(Row, Column) samples Source at X = Map_X(Row, Column) (source column) and Y = Map_Y(Row, Column) (source row). Relative maps are not bound. Source must be a nonempty 2-D Mat of depth UInt8, UInt16, Int16, Float32, or Float64 with 1 to 4 channels and with Rows and Columns less than 32767. Map_X and Map_Y must be nonempty 2-D single-channel Float32 Mats of identical size, each with Rows and Columns less than 32767; map geometry need not match Source. Every map coordinate V must be finite and safely convertible through native integer coordinate tables: abs (V) < 2.0**31 for Nearest_Neighbor and abs (32.0 * V) < 2.0**31 for the other methods, evaluated in Float32. Coordinates may lie outside Source; Border then supplies the samples.

The result is computed privately; only after native Remap succeeds is Destination replaced with fresh storage of Rows = Map_X.Rows, Columns = Map_X.Columns, and Source's depth and channel count. Callers do not need to preallocate Destination, and failed calls leave it unchanged. No color conversion occurs. Source, Map_X, and Map_Y are read-only and may share storage. Destination must not share storage with Source, Map_X, or Map_Y; the same Mat or the same data start is rejected. Direct in-place operation is not supported. Native stride limits also raise OpenCV_Error: a UInt8 C1/C3/C4 Linear Source row stride above 2**31 - 1 or exactly 32768 bytes, and, for Constant_Border calls that OpenCV 4.x may route to IPP, Source, map or result byte strides above 2**31 - 1.

Parameters
Source

Image to sample.

Map_X

Source column (X) coordinate for each Destination pixel.

Map_Y

Source row (Y) coordinate for each Destination pixel.

Destination

Receives the remapped image.

Interpolation

Nearest_Neighbor, Linear (the default), Cubic, or Lanczos_4; Area and Linear_Exact are rejected.

Border

Any Border_Kind value; default Constant_Border.

Border_Value

Fill value for Constant_Border: C1 uses Component_0, C2 uses Components 0..1, C3 uses 0..2, and C4 uses 0..3. Ignored for other borders. Default all zero.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Invalid Source or maps, unsafe map coordinate or stride, unsupported Interpolation, Destination sharing storage with an input, or an OpenCV failure.

Remap

procedure Remap
  (Source        : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Map_XY        : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination   : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Interpolation : Interpolation_Method := Linear;
   Border        : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Constant_Border;
   Border_Value  : OpenCV.Scalar := (others => 0.0))

Remap with one absolute Float32 C2 map, which stores X (source column) in component 0 and Y (source row) in component 1. It must be nonempty, two-dimensional, and have both dimensions < 32767; its coordinates obey the finiteness and rounding limits of the separate-map overload. On success Destination is replaced with fresh storage of Map_XY's rows and columns and Source's depth and channel count. The Source, interpolation, borders, alias, UInt8 Linear stride and failure contracts of the separate-map overload also apply; its IPP stride checks do not.

Parameters
Source

Image to sample.

Map_XY

Source (X, Y) coordinates for each Destination pixel.

Destination

Receives the remapped image.

Interpolation

Nearest_Neighbor, Linear (the default), Cubic, or Lanczos_4; Area and Linear_Exact are rejected.

Border

Any Border_Kind value; default Constant_Border.

Border_Value

Fill value for Constant_Border, used per channel as in the separate-map overload; ignored for other borders.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Invalid Source or Map_XY, unsafe map coordinate or stride, unsupported Interpolation, Destination sharing storage with an input, or an OpenCV failure.

Remap

procedure Remap
  (Source        : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Maps          : Fixed_Remap_Maps;
   Destination   : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Interpolation : Interpolation_Method := Linear;
   Border        : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Constant_Border;
   Border_Value  : OpenCV.Scalar := (others => 0.0))

Remap with fixed maps from Convert_Remap_To_Fixed, which can be reused for many frames. Coefficient-bearing maps support Nearest_Neighbor, Linear, Cubic and Lanczos_4; nearest-only maps support Nearest_Neighbor exclusively, and empty maps are rejected. On success Destination is replaced with fresh storage of the maps' rows and columns and Source's depth and channel count. The Source, border, alias, UInt8 Linear stride and failure contracts of the separate-map overload also apply; its Float32 coordinate and IPP stride checks do not.

Parameters
Source

Image to sample.

Maps

Fixed source-coordinate maps.

Destination

Receives the remapped image.

Interpolation

Nearest_Neighbor, Linear (the default), Cubic, or Lanczos_4 for coefficient-bearing maps; only Nearest_Neighbor for nearest-only maps. Area and Linear_Exact are rejected.

Border

Any Border_Kind value; default Constant_Border.

Border_Value

Fill value for Constant_Border, used per channel as in the separate-map overload; ignored for other borders.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Invalid Source, empty or invalid Maps, interpolation not supported by Maps, unsafe stride, Destination sharing storage with an input, or an OpenCV failure.

Resize

procedure Resize
  (Source        : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination   : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Output_Size   : OpenCV.Size;
   Interpolation : Interpolation_Method := Linear)

Resamples Source to Output_Size with native cv::resize. The result has Output_Size.Height rows, Output_Size.Width columns and Source's depth and channel count; no color conversion occurs. Source must be a nonempty 2-D Mat of depth UInt8, UInt16, Int16, Float32 or Float64, and Output_Size must have nonzero width and height. Same-size resizing copies the source.

Destination is the direct native output: OpenCV reuses its storage when it already has the result size and type (shallow copies of Destination and a Region's parent then see the result) and otherwise rebinds it to new storage. Ada validation and the Linear_Exact layout preflight raise before Destination is touched.

Linear_Exact is Resize-only fixed-point bilinear interpolation for UInt8, UInt16, and Int16, with deterministic integer rounding and any channel count. It is not lossless or mathematically exact, and can differ from Linear. Float32/Float64 exact requests are rejected rather than silently falling back to Linear. Native exact 2x downscaling in both axes may use bit-equivalent Area (except for C2). An exact Region source uses only its logical pixels. Native signed coefficient, line-buffer and allocation limits of exact resizing raise OpenCV_Error.

Parameters
Source

Image to resample.

Destination

Receives the resized image.

Output_Size

Result columns (Width) and rows (Height).

Interpolation

Resampling method; every Interpolation_Method value is accepted, Linear_Exact only for integer sources. Default Linear.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Empty, non-2-D or unsupported-depth Source, zero Output_Size dimension, Float32/Float64 Linear_Exact request, exact layout overflow, or an OpenCV failure.

Restore_GrabCut_State

function Restore_GrabCut_State
  (Mask             : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Background_Model : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Foreground_Model : OpenCV.Core.Mat) return GrabCut_State

Import learned GrabCut state without running segmentation. Mask must be UInt8 C1, 2-D, nonempty, with labels 0 .. 3. Each model must be Float64 C1, 1 x 65, with a valid native GrabCut GMM payload. All inputs are deep-cloned; sparse classes are allowed in restored masks. A valid payload is finite, has nonnegative weights and at least one active (positive-weight) component, and every active covariance has a determinant and inverse usable by the native mixture model; inactive components may hold stale finite values. Later refinement requires a Source with Mask's rows and columns.

Parameters
Mask

Label mask, for example from GrabCut_Mask.

Background_Model

Background mixture model, for example from GrabCut_Background_Model.

Foreground_Model

Foreground mixture model, for example from GrabCut_Foreground_Model.

Return Value

New initialized state owning deep copies of the three Mats.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised when Mask is not a nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1 Mat of labels 0 .. 3, or when a model has the wrong shape or type or an invalid payload.

Scharr

procedure Scharr
  (Source            : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination       : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Axis              : Derivative_Axis;
   Destination_Depth : Derivative_Depth := Float32_Depth;
   Scale             : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 1.0;
   Offset            : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 0.0;
   Border            : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Reflect_101)

Scharr computes the first spatial derivative in Axis independently for every Source channel (native cv::Scharr) with the 3x3 Scharr kernel whose separable factors Get_Scharr_Kernels returns: [-1, 0, 1] along Axis and [3, 10, 3] across it. It stores Scale * derivative + Offset. Its source, destination-depth, Scale, Delta (Offset), and Border requirements are the same as Sobel's. Destination is a direct native output with Source's rows, columns, and channel count: storage that already has the result's size and type is reused (shallow copies and a Region's parent see the result); otherwise Destination is rebound to new storage. OpenCV supports direct in-place use only when Destination_Depth is Same_Depth; Source remains unchanged when Destination is distinct.

Parameters
Source

Image to differentiate.

Destination

Receives the scaled derivative image.

Axis

Direction of the first derivative.

Destination_Depth

Output depth; Float32_Depth by default (see Derivative_Depth for the supported combinations).

Scale

Factor applied to each derivative value; must be finite.

Offset

Value added after scaling (OpenCV's delta); must be finite.

Border

Extrapolation outside Source: Constant_Border, Replicate, Reflect, or Reflect_101 (default). Wrap is rejected.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D, or unsupported Source, an unsupported depth combination, a nonfinite Scale or Offset, Wrap, or a failure reported by OpenCV.

Sep_Filter_2D

procedure Sep_Filter_2D
  (Source            : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination       : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Kernel_X          : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Kernel_Y          : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination_Depth : Filter_Depth := Same_Depth;
   Offset            : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 0.0;
   Border            : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Reflect_101)

Sep_Filter_2D replaces Destination with a separable linear filter of Source (native cv::sepFilter2D). Every Source row is filtered with Kernel_X, then every intermediate column is filtered with Kernel_Y, and Offset is added to the final value. Coefficient order is preserved; neither kernel is flipped. Source must be a non-empty two-dimensional Mat of depth UInt8, UInt16, Int16, Float32, or Float64. Channel count is unrestricted; the same Kernel_X and Kernel_Y pair is applied independently to every Source channel. Kernel lengths need not be odd, equal, normalized, or symmetric. Kernel_X and Kernel_Y must share the same floating-point depth because native OpenCV requires a common kernel type; they may share storage with each other. This overload uses OpenCV's centered default anchor; there is no public (-1, -1) sentinel.

Destination receives Source's rows, columns, and channel count, with the requested depth, as a direct native output: storage that already has that size and type is reused (shallow copies and a Region's parent see the result); otherwise Destination is rebound to new storage. Direct in-place operation is supported when the requested destination depth matches Source; a depth-changing alias (Destination is Source or starts at its first element) is rejected. Source remains unchanged when Destination is distinct.

Parameters
Source

Image to filter.

Destination

Receives the filtered image.

Kernel_X

Row (X) coefficients: a non-empty two-dimensional single-channel Float32 or Float64 vector, one row or one column. It must not share Destination's storage.

Kernel_Y

Column (Y) coefficients with the same requirements and the same depth as Kernel_X.

Destination_Depth

Output depth, Same_Depth by default, using the same supported source/destination combinations as Filter_2D (see Derivative_Depth).

Offset

Value added to each filtered value; must be finite.

Border

Extrapolation outside Source: Constant_Border, Replicate, Reflect, or Reflect_101 (default). Wrap is rejected.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D, or unsupported Source, a kernel that is not a non-empty Float32 or Float64 C1 vector, kernels of different depths, an unsupported depth combination, a nonfinite Offset, Wrap, a rejected alias, or a failure reported by OpenCV.

Sep_Filter_2D

procedure Sep_Filter_2D
  (Source            : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination       : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Kernel_X          : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Kernel_Y          : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Anchor            : OpenCV.Point;
   Destination_Depth : Filter_Depth := Same_Depth;
   Offset            : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 0.0;
   Border            : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Reflect_101)

This overload is the same Sep_Filter_2D separable filter with an explicit Kernel Anchor. Anchor.X indexes Kernel_X and Anchor.Y indexes Kernel_Y using each kernel's logical length (columns if it is a row vector, otherwise rows). Both coordinates must be nonnegative and strictly less than the corresponding logical length. There is no public mixed or centered (-1, -1) sentinel in this overload. Source, kernel, depth, Offset, border, and direct native Destination rules match the centered overload. Direct in-place operation is supported when the requested destination depth matches Source.

Parameters
Source

Image to filter.

Destination

Receives the filtered image.

Kernel_X

Row (X) coefficients: a non-empty single-channel Float32 or Float64 row or column vector, not sharing Destination's storage.

Kernel_Y

Column (Y) coefficients with the same requirements and the same depth as Kernel_X.

Anchor

Index into Kernel_X (X) and Kernel_Y (Y) of the coefficient aligned with each output pixel.

Destination_Depth

Output depth, Same_Depth by default; see Derivative_Depth for the supported combinations.

Offset

Value added to each filtered value; must be finite.

Border

Extrapolation outside Source: Constant_Border, Replicate, Reflect, or Reflect_101 (default). Wrap is rejected.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an Anchor coordinate outside its kernel, any input rejected by the centered overload, or a failure reported by OpenCV.

Separate_Remap_Map

function Separate_Remap_Map
  (Map_XY : OpenCV.Core.Mat) return Float_Remap_Maps

Splits a Float32 C2 map into separate Float32 C1 maps: component 0 becomes Map_X and component 1 Map_Y. Map_XY must be nonempty and 2-D with dimensions < 32767; values are copied without a coordinate check. Map_XY is unchanged.

Parameters
Map_XY

Interleaved source (X, Y) coordinates.

Return Value

Fresh owning Float32 C1 maps with Map_XY's geometry.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Invalid map type or geometry, or an OpenCV failure.

Sobel

procedure Sobel
  (Source            : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination       : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   X_Order           : Derivative_Order;
   Y_Order           : Derivative_Order;
   Destination_Depth : Derivative_Depth := Float32_Depth;
   Kernel_Size       : Sobel_Kernel_Size := Kernel_3;
   Scale             : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 1.0;
   Offset            : OpenCV.Float64_Value := 0.0;
   Border            : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Reflect_101)

Sobel computes the X_Order/Y_Order spatial derivative independently for every Source channel (native cv::Sobel) and stores Scale * derivative + Offset, where Offset is OpenCV's delta term. Source must be a non-empty two-dimensional UInt8, UInt16, Int16, Float32, or Float64 Mat. X_Order and Y_Order may not both be zero; each order must be less than Kernel_Size, except Kernel_1 uses OpenCV's special effective three-tap derivative kernel.

Destination has Source's rows, columns, and channel count, with depth selected by Destination_Depth: UInt8 supports Same_Depth, Int16_Depth, Float32_Depth, and Float64_Depth. UInt16 and Int16 support Same_Depth, Float32_Depth, and Float64_Depth. Float32 supports Same_Depth or Float32_Depth; Float64 supports Same_Depth or Float64_Depth. A UInt8 Same_Depth result cannot hold negative derivatives. Destination is a direct native output: storage that already has the result's size and type is reused (shallow copies and a Region's parent see the result); otherwise Destination is rebound to new storage. OpenCV supports direct in-place use only when Destination_Depth is Same_Depth; Source remains unchanged when Destination is distinct. A Region Source is passed to OpenCV directly, and native Sobel may inspect parent pixels around the Region; pass a Clone to isolate it.

Parameters
Source

Image to differentiate.

Destination

Receives the scaled derivative image.

X_Order

Derivative order along X (columns).

Y_Order

Derivative order along Y (rows).

Destination_Depth

Output depth; Float32_Depth by default.

Kernel_Size

Sobel aperture; Kernel_3 by default.

Scale

Factor applied to each derivative value; must be finite.

Offset

Value added after scaling (OpenCV's delta); must be finite.

Border

Extrapolation outside Source: Constant_Border, Replicate, Reflect, or Reflect_101 (default). Wrap is not supported.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D, or unsupported Source, an unsupported depth combination, both orders zero or an order not below the effective kernel length, a nonfinite Scale or Offset, Wrap, or a failure reported by OpenCV.

Sobel_Kernel_Size

type Sobel_Kernel_Size is (Kernel_1, Kernel_3, Kernel_5, Kernel_7);

Aperture of the Sobel-family derivative kernels used by Sobel and Get_Derivative_Kernels. A derivative order along an axis must be less than the effective kernel length on that axis.

Enumeration Literal
Kernel_1

OpenCV's special case without smoothing: an axis with nonzero order (up to 2) uses an effective 3-tap derivative kernel, such as [-1, 0, 1], and an axis with order zero a 1-tap identity.

Kernel_3

Three-tap kernels on both axes (orders up to 2).

Kernel_5

Five-tap kernels on both axes (orders up to 4).

Kernel_7

Seven-tap kernels on both axes (orders up to 6).

Spatial_Gradient

function Spatial_Gradient
  (Source : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Border : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Reflect_101)
   return Spatial_Gradient_Result

Native fixed 3x3 first-order Sobel pair (cv::spatialGradient), with no kernel-size selector. Source must be nonempty, exactly 2-D UInt8 C1. Each result is a fresh independent Int16 C1 Mat with Source geometry and mathematical range -1020 .. 1020, so no saturation or wrap occurs. Source remains unchanged. Regions are logical images: parent pixels never participate. A one-column Source is computed through a private two-column copy with the same Sobel semantics, avoiding an unsafe native read.

Parameters
Source

UInt8 C1 image to differentiate.

Border

Extrapolation outside Source: Reflect_101 (default) or Replicate only.

Return Value

Fresh X_Derivative/Y_Derivative pair.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an invalid Source, any other Border, or a native failure.

Spatial_Gradient_Result

type Spatial_Gradient_Result is record
   X_Derivative : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Y_Derivative : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
end record;

First-derivative pair returned by Spatial_Gradient. Each component is a fresh independent Int16 C1 Mat with the Source geometry, sharing storage with neither Source nor the other component.

Record fields
X_Derivative

Horizontal derivative: 3x3 Sobel with X order 1.

Y_Derivative

Vertical derivative: 3x3 Sobel with Y order 1.

Squared_Box_Filter

function Squared_Box_Filter
  (Source      : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Kernel_Size : OpenCV.Size;
   Mode        : Squared_Box_Mode := Mean_Square;
   Border      : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Reflect_101)
   return OpenCV.Core.Mat

Native squared box filtering (cv::sqrBoxFilter): for every pixel and channel, sums the squares of the Source samples in a Kernel_Size window and, for Mean_Square, divides by the effective kernel area. Returns fresh independent Float64 storage with Source's rows, columns and channel count; Source is unchanged.

Source must be nonempty 2-D UInt8 or Float32 with 1 or 3 channels. The window for column X starts at X - Width / 2 and spans Width columns, and rows likewise, so positive odd, even and oversized kernels are all accepted. Normalized nonconstant singleton axes reduce to one: with Mean_Square and a Border other than Constant_Border, a one-column or one-row Source uses a kernel dimension of one on that axis; Constant_Border and Sum_Of_Squares keep the requested dimensions. The UInt8 effective area is at most 33025 (native signed accumulator) and the Float32 area at most 2_147_483_647; other unsafe native signed arithmetic is rejected.

Regions are isolated: only the Region's logical pixels are read and border extrapolation never reaches the parent. Float32 retains native incremental rounding and NaN/Inf propagation, which can persist beyond the ideal mathematical local window; tiny finite residuals after large values may be lost. This is not an exact per-window sum.

Parameters
Source

Image whose squared samples are summed.

Kernel_Size

Window width and height in pixels; both must be positive and may be even or exceed the image.

Mode

Mean_Square (default) divides by the effective area; Sum_Of_Squares returns the plain sum.

Border

Extrapolation outside Source: Constant_Border (zero samples, still counted in the Mean_Square area), Replicate, Reflect or Reflect_101 (default). Wrap is rejected.

Return Value

Fresh owning Float64 Mat with one squared-window value per Source pixel and channel.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an empty, non-2-D or unsupported Source, a zero kernel dimension, Wrap, an effective area above its limit, unsafe native arithmetic, or a native failure.

Squared_Box_Mode

type Squared_Box_Mode is (Mean_Square, Sum_Of_Squares);

Normalization Squared_Box_Filter applies to the sum of squared samples in each window.

Enumeration Literal
Mean_Square

Divide the window sum by the effective kernel area Width * Height, giving the local mean of the squares.

Sum_Of_Squares

Return the unnormalized window sum of squares.

Subpixel_Patch_Output_Depth

type Subpixel_Patch_Output_Depth is
  (Preserve_Source_Depth, Promote_To_Float32);

Result depth of Extract_Subpixel_Patch.

Enumeration Literal
Preserve_Source_Depth

The patch has Source's depth, UInt8 or Float32 (the default).

Promote_To_Float32

The patch is Float32: UInt8 sources are promoted and Float32 sources remain Float32.

Template_Matching_Method

type Template_Matching_Method is
  (Squared_Difference,
   Normalized_Squared_Difference,
   Cross_Correlation,
   Normalized_Cross_Correlation,
   Correlation_Coefficient,
   Normalized_Correlation_Coefficient);

Comparison computed by Match_Template at every placement of Template over Source, as the corresponding OpenCV TM_* selector. Scores are Float32 values with native rounding. Use OpenCV.Core.Min_Max_Loc on the score map: the best match is the minimum for the two squared-difference methods and the maximum for the other four. The masked Match_Template overload does not support Normalized_Correlation_Coefficient and documents its masked formulas and zero-energy rules.

Enumeration Literal
Squared_Difference

TM_SQDIFF: sum of squared Template and window differences, ideally zero for a perfect match. Lower is better: the best match is the minimum.

Normalized_Squared_Difference

TM_SQDIFF_NORMED: squared difference divided by sqrt (template energy * window energy). Lower is better: the best match is the minimum.

Cross_Correlation

TM_CCORR: unnormalized sum of Template and window products, growing with brightness. Higher is better: the best match is the maximum.

Normalized_Cross_Correlation

TM_CCORR_NORMED: cosine similarity of Template and window. Higher is better: the best match is the maximum.

Correlation_Coefficient

TM_CCOEFF: mean-centered correlation (the covariance numerator); it is not scale-normalized and not limited to [-1, 1]. Higher is better: the best match is the maximum.

Normalized_Correlation_Coefficient

TM_CCOEFF_NORMED: mean-centered and scale-normalized correlation coefficient. Higher is better: the best match is the maximum. Unmasked overload only.

Text_Font

type Text_Font is
  (Hershey_Simplex,
   Hershey_Plain,
   Hershey_Duplex,
   Hershey_Complex,
   Hershey_Triplex,
   Hershey_Complex_Small,
   Hershey_Script_Simplex,
   Hershey_Script_Complex);

Legacy Hershey font selector for Draw_Text, Measure_Text and Font_Scale_For_Height. Each literal selects the OpenCV Hershey font at the same position, from FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX through FONT_HERSHEY_SCRIPT_COMPLEX. OpenCV 4.1 and 4.10 render classic Hershey strokes; OpenCV 5.0 maps the same selectors to built-in sans-serif, serif and italic TrueType faces, so glyphs and exact metrics can differ between generations. The legacy italic flag is not exposed.

Enumeration Literal
Hershey_Simplex

Normal-size sans-serif font; the default.

Hershey_Plain

Small sans-serif font.

Hershey_Duplex

Normal-size sans-serif font, more complex than Hershey_Simplex.

Hershey_Complex

Normal-size serif font.

Hershey_Triplex

Normal-size serif font, more complex than Hershey_Complex.

Hershey_Complex_Small

Smaller version of Hershey_Complex.

Hershey_Script_Simplex

Hand-writing style font.

Hershey_Script_Complex

More complex variant of Hershey_Script_Simplex.

Text_Metrics

type Text_Metrics is record
   Size     : OpenCV.Size;
   Baseline : Natural;
end record;

Native text extent reported by Measure_Text, in pixels.

Record fields
Size

Width and Height of the text's bounding box as reported by OpenCV for the font, scale and thickness; (0, 0) for empty text.

Baseline

Native baseline value, unadjusted by the binding: the distance from the bottom-most text point up to the text baseline. Zero for empty text. OpenCV's layout example adds the thickness to it for padding; the binding does not.

Threshold_Mode

type Threshold_Mode is
  (Binary, Binary_Inverse, Truncate, To_Zero, To_Zero_Inverse);

Per-sample rule of Apply_Threshold and Apply_Automatic_Threshold for a Source sample S, threshold T and maximum value M. The comparison is strict: a sample equal to T counts as not above it.

Enumeration Literal
Binary

M where S > T, otherwise 0.

Binary_Inverse

0 where S > T, otherwise M.

Truncate

T where S > T, otherwise S unchanged.

To_Zero

S unchanged where S > T, otherwise 0.

To_Zero_Inverse

0 where S > T, otherwise S unchanged.

Update_Running_Average

function Update_Running_Average
  (Source, Base, Mask : OpenCV.Core.Mat; Weight : OpenCV.Float64_Value)
   return OpenCV.Core.Mat

Masked Update_Running_Average: the result is (1 - Weight) * Base + Weight * Source only at pixels whose Mask sample is nonzero and Base elsewhere. Any nonzero Mask value enables every channel at that pixel. Otherwise as the unmasked overload.

Parameters
Source

UInt8 or Float32 C1 or C3 image blended into Base at enabled pixels.

Base

Float32 or Float64 running average of Source's geometry and channel count. It is read, not updated.

Mask

Nonempty 2-D UInt8 C1 selection with Source's rows and columns; zero preserves Base. It may share storage with Source.

Weight

Finite weight of Source in 0.0 .. 1.0.

Return Value

Fresh Mat typed like Base holding the masked average.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for an invalid Weight, Source or Base as for the unmasked overload, or for a Mask that is empty, not 2-D, not UInt8 C1 or of different geometry.

Update_Running_Average

function Update_Running_Average
  (Source, Base : OpenCV.Core.Mat; Weight : OpenCV.Float64_Value)
   return OpenCV.Core.Mat

Running (exponentially weighted) average: the result is (1 - Weight) * Base + Weight * Source at every pixel and channel, in fresh storage typed like Base. Weight must be finite and in 0.0 .. 1.0 (otherwise OpenCV_Error). Zero retains a finite Base; one yields the source values converted to Base's depth. Native code may round Weight to the accumulator precision or, on IPP paths, to Float32. Source is UInt8 or Float32 C1 or C3; Base is Float32 or Float64 with Source's geometry and channel count. Otherwise as Accumulate_Image: nonempty 2-D inputs, private Base clone published only after success, logical Regions, all aliases supported, native layout limits, and unsaturated IEEE floating arithmetic.

Parameters
Source

UInt8 or Float32 C1 or C3 image blended into Base.

Base

Float32 or Float64 running average of Source's geometry and channel count. It is read, not updated.

Weight

Finite weight of Source in 0.0 .. 1.0; larger values forget Base faster.

Return Value

Fresh Mat typed like Base holding the updated average.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised for a NaN, infinite or out-of-range Weight, an empty or non-2-D input, mismatched geometry or channel count, an unsupported depth or channel count, an input beyond the native layout limits, or a native failure.

Warp_Affine

procedure Warp_Affine
  (Source        : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Transform     : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination   : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Output_Size   : OpenCV.Size;
   Interpolation : Interpolation_Method := Linear;
   Mapping       : Affine_Mapping_Direction := Source_To_Destination;
   Border        : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Constant_Border;
   Border_Value  : OpenCV.Scalar := (others => 0.0))

Warp_Affine applies a 2x3 affine Transform to Source with native cv::warpAffine and writes the warped image to Destination. Source must be a nonempty 2-D Mat of depth UInt8, UInt16, Int16, Float32, or Float64 with 1 to 4 channels. Transform must be a nonempty 2-D 2x3 single-channel Mat of depth Float32 or Float64 whose six coefficients are all finite:

[ M00 M01 M02 ]
[ M10 M11 M12 ]

The result has Rows = Output_Size.Height, Columns = Output_Size.Width, and Source's depth and channel count; its geometry is not inferred from Source and no color conversion occurs. Destination is the direct native output: OpenCV reuses its storage when it already has that size and type (shallow copies of Destination and a Region's parent then see the result) and otherwise rebinds it to new storage, so callers do not need to preallocate it. Rejected arguments leave Destination unchanged. Source and Transform are read-only and may share storage. Destination must not share storage with Source or Transform; the same Mat or the same data start is rejected. Direct in-place operation is not supported.

Parameters
Source

Image to warp.

Transform

2x3 affine coefficient matrix, interpreted according to Mapping.

Destination

Receives the warped image.

Output_Size

Result columns (Width) and rows (Height); both must be nonzero.

Interpolation

Nearest_Neighbor or Linear (the default); Cubic, Area, Lanczos_4 and Linear_Exact are rejected.

Mapping

Source_To_Destination (the default) is the usual forward mapping; Destination_To_Source treats Transform as already inverted.

Border

Constant_Border (the default) or Replicate; Reflect, Reflect_101 and Wrap are rejected.

Border_Value

Fill value for Constant_Border: C1 uses Component_0, C2 uses Components 0..1, C3 uses 0..2, and C4 uses 0..3. Ignored for Replicate. Default all zero.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Invalid Source or Transform, nonfinite coefficient, zero Output_Size dimension, unsupported Interpolation or Border, Destination sharing storage with an input, or an OpenCV failure.

Warp_Mapping_Direction

type Warp_Mapping_Direction is
  (Source_To_Destination, Destination_To_Source);

How Warp_Affine and Warp_Perspective interpret their Transform.

Enumeration Literal
Source_To_Destination

Transform maps Source coordinates to Destination coordinates: the usual forward warp, which OpenCV inverts internally. A +1 X translation moves content right.

Destination_To_Source

Transform is already inverted and maps each Destination pixel to the Source position it samples (native WARP_INVERSE_MAP). A +1 X translation moves content left.

Warp_Perspective

procedure Warp_Perspective
  (Source        : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Transform     : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination   : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Output_Size   : OpenCV.Size;
   Interpolation : Interpolation_Method := Linear;
   Mapping       : Warp_Mapping_Direction := Source_To_Destination;
   Border        : OpenCV.Border_Kind := OpenCV.Constant_Border;
   Border_Value  : OpenCV.Scalar := (others => 0.0))

Warp_Perspective applies a 3x3 projective Transform to Source with native cv::warpPerspective and writes the warped image to Destination. Source must be a nonempty 2-D Mat of depth UInt8, UInt16, Int16, Float32, or Float64 with 1 to 4 channels. Transform must be a nonempty 2-D 3x3 single-channel Mat of depth Float32 or Float64:

[ M00 M01 M02 ]
[ M10 M11 M12 ]
[ M20 M21 M22 ]

With Destination_To_Source, Destination pixel (x, y) samples Source at

X = (M00 * x + M01 * y + M02) / (M20 * x + M21 * y + M22)
Y = (M10 * x + M11 * y + M12) / (M20 * x + M21 * y + M22)

Singular or poorly conditioned matrices are not rejected merely for being singular; all nine coefficients must be finite. The result has Rows = Output_Size.Height, Columns = Output_Size.Width, and Source's depth and channel count; no color conversion occurs. Destination is the direct native output: OpenCV reuses its storage when it already has that size and type (shallow copies of Destination and a Region's parent then see the result) and otherwise rebinds it to new storage, so callers do not need to preallocate it. Rejected arguments leave Destination unchanged. Source and Transform are read-only and may share storage. Destination must not share storage with Source or Transform; the same Mat or the same data start is rejected. Direct in-place operation is not supported.

Parameters
Source

Image to warp.

Transform

3x3 projective coefficient matrix, interpreted according to Mapping.

Destination

Receives the warped image.

Output_Size

Result columns (Width) and rows (Height); both must be nonzero.

Interpolation

Nearest_Neighbor or Linear (the default); Cubic, Area, Lanczos_4 and Linear_Exact are rejected.

Mapping

Source_To_Destination (the default) is the usual forward mapping; Destination_To_Source treats Transform as already inverted.

Border

Constant_Border (the default) or Replicate; Reflect, Reflect_101 and Wrap are rejected.

Border_Value

Fill value for Constant_Border: C1 uses Component_0, C2 uses Components 0..1, C3 uses 0..2, and C4 uses 0..3. Ignored for Replicate. Default all zero.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Invalid Source or Transform, nonfinite coefficient, zero Output_Size dimension, unsupported Interpolation or Border, Destination sharing storage with an input, or an OpenCV failure.

Warp_Polar

procedure Warp_Polar
  (Source         : OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Destination    : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat;
   Center         : OpenCV.Float32_Point;
   Maximum_Radius : OpenCV.Float64_Value;
   Output_Size    : OpenCV.Size;
   Mapping        : Polar_Mapping := Linear_Polar;
   Direction      : Polar_Direction := Cartesian_To_Polar;
   Interpolation  : Interpolation_Method := Linear)

Converts between Cartesian and polar images with native cv::warpPolar. Polar output X is radial rho and Y is angular phi (one revolution). Linear rho follows distance; logarithmic rho follows log(distance + 1). Maximum_Radius (or its logarithm) spans the polar image width: Output_Size.Width in forward mode, Source.Columns in inverse mode. In forward mode Center is relative to the Source view (Region-local); in inverse mode Source is the polar image and Center is relative to the requested Cartesian destination. Off-image centers are allowed within the generated-map arithmetic limits.

Source must be nonempty 2-D UInt8/UInt16/Int16/Float32/Float64, C1..C4, with Rows and Columns < 32767; inverse Source.Rows must be <= 32764 because OpenCV adds a wrap row at each end. In inverse mode those wrap rows come from the Region itself, never its parent. Outliers are zero-filled. Generated Float32 map coordinates are preflighted against native remap rounding limits, so extreme centers or radii, tiny inverse logarithmic scales and unsafe source strides are rejected.

Destination must not overlap Source, including Regions and aliases. Successful calls replace Destination with a fresh result of Output_Size and Source's type, computed privately and published only on success; failed calls leave it unchanged. Round trips are interpolated and are not guaranteed to reproduce the input exactly.

Parameters
Source

Cartesian image (forward) or polar image (inverse).

Destination

Receives the transformed image.

Center

Transform center in pixels (X = column, Y = row); must be finite.

Maximum_Radius

Source radius mapped across the polar width; must be finite and > 0 (linear) or > 1 (logarithmic).

Output_Size

Explicit result columns (Width) and rows (Height), each nonzero and < 32767. In forward mode Width counts rho samples and Height angle samples.

Mapping

Linear_Polar (the default) or Logarithmic_Polar.

Direction

Cartesian_To_Polar (the default) or Polar_To_Cartesian.

Interpolation

Nearest_Neighbor, Linear (the default), Cubic or Lanczos_4; Area and Linear_Exact are rejected.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Invalid Source type or size, invalid Output_Size, nonfinite Center, invalid Maximum_Radius, unsupported Interpolation, unsafe generated coordinates or strides, Destination overlapping Source, or an OpenCV failure.

Watershed

procedure Watershed
  (Source : OpenCV.Core.Mat; Markers : in out OpenCV.Core.Mat)

Marker-based watershed. Source is a nonempty two-dimensional UInt8 C3 image and is not modified. Markers is an Int32 C1 Mat of the same rows and columns, updated in place: on input 0 means unknown and each positive value seeds a region (negative input values are rejected); on output unknown pixels carry a propagated region label, and -1 marks watershed boundaries, including Markers' one-pixel outer border. Markers must not share storage with Source.

Markers is mutated directly, never cloned: shallow copies observe the labels, and a Markers Region writes through to its parent while parent elements outside the Region stay unchanged (the Region's own outer border becomes -1). Strides and pixel count must fit OpenCV's signed-int offset and node-pool arithmetic. Validation failures leave Markers unchanged.

Parameters
Source

Nonempty 2-D UInt8 C3 image whose colours guide the flooding.

Markers

Int32 C1 seed labels on input and region labels with -1 boundaries on output, updated in place.

Raised exceptions
OpenCV_Error

Raised when Source is not a nonempty 2-D UInt8 C3 image, when Markers is not a nonempty 2-D Int32 C1 Mat of Source's rows and columns or holds a negative value, when Markers shares storage with Source, for a native size limit, or when OpenCV fails.

YUV420_Color_Output

type YUV420_Color_Output is
  (BGR_Output, RGB_Output, BGRA_Output, RGBA_Output);

Channel layout of a decoded YUV 4:2:0 or 4:2:2 frame: a UInt8 image with the frame's H rows and W columns.

Enumeration Literal
BGR_Output

C3 in B, G, R order.

RGB_Output

C3 in R, G, B order.

BGRA_Output

C4 in B, G, R, alpha order; alpha is always 255.

RGBA_Output

C4 in R, G, B, alpha order; alpha is always 255.

YUV420_Layout

type YUV420_Layout is (I420, YV12, NV12, NV21);

Plane order of a YUV 4:2:0 frame of logical W columns and H rows, with one Y byte per pixel and one U and one V byte per 2x2 pixel block. The packed UInt8 C1 form (W columns, H + H/2 rows) is read as one row-major byte stream that starts with the W x H Y plane; U and V each occupy W * H / 4 bytes.

Enumeration Literal
I420

Planar: the Y plane, then the U plane, then the V plane.

YV12

Planar: the Y plane, then the V plane, then the U plane.

NV12

Semiplanar: the Y plane, then interleaved U,V byte pairs.

NV21

Semiplanar: the Y plane, then interleaved V,U byte pairs.

YUV420_Planar_Layout

subtype YUV420_Planar_Layout is YUV420_Layout range I420 .. YV12;

The planar layouts I420 and YV12, the only ones Encode_YUV420_Planar produces; NV12/NV21 encoding is not provided.

YUV420_Semiplanar_Layout

subtype YUV420_Semiplanar_Layout is YUV420_Layout range NV12 .. NV21;

The semiplanar layouts NV12 and NV21, whose chroma is one interleaved plane; the only layouts Decode_YUV420_Two_Plane accepts.

YUV422_Color_Output

subtype YUV422_Color_Output is YUV420_Color_Output;

Result layout of Decode_YUV422: the YUV420_Color_Output literals with the same meaning, C3 BGR/RGB or C4 BGRA/RGBA with alpha 255.

YUV422_Layout

type YUV422_Layout is (UYVY, YUY2, YVYU);

Byte order of a packed YUV 4:2:2 frame stored as UInt8 C2. Each C2 element holds two consecutive stream bytes, and the elements at columns 2k and 2k+1 of a row hold two horizontal pixels, Y0 and Y1, sharing one U and one V sample. VYUY and planar 4:2:2 are not supported.

Enumeration Literal
UYVY

Stream U, Y0, V, Y1: elements (U, Y0) and (V, Y1); Y is channel 1.

YUY2

Stream Y0, U, Y1, V: elements (Y0, U) and (Y1, V); Y is channel 0.

YVYU

Stream Y0, V, Y1, U: elements (Y0, V) and (Y1, U); Y is channel 0.

YUV_Color_Order

type YUV_Color_Order is (BGR_Order, RGB_Order);

Channel order of the color Source of Encode_YUV420_Planar. A C4 Source's alpha is ignored, not premultiplied.

Enumeration Literal
BGR_Order

Source is BGR (C3) or BGRA (C4).

RGB_Order

Source is RGB (C3) or RGBA (C4).
